- **How (Shepherd):** Emit a new ledger event at the existing detection edge in claude-dashboard.lua ~5427: where `core.transcriptError(tail)` flips `it.status="error"`, add `ledgerFor(it, {type="error", error_type=<classified>, error_message=err.message})` — but only on the transition edge (fire once when prev.status was not "error", reusing the `prev[key]` one-row pattern so a tile stuck in error doesn't append every ~1s refresh). Extend `core.transcriptError` (cc-core.lua:1588) to also return a coarse `error_type` (e.g. overloaded_error / connection / rate_limit) parsed from `obj.error`, the only error-shaped data a local session exposes. Add `"error"` to the default ledger event-type allow-list and the filter overlay (the `splitLedgerEvents`/types path, cc-core.lua ~523). Teach `M.fleetStats` (cc-core.lua:954) to add an `errors`/`failedTurns` bucket and per-session error counts, and surface a "failed turns" line in the shift report. Drop all proxy-only fields (status_code, api_base, model_group, request_kwargs, request_id, traceback-suppression env) — they are unobservable to Shepherd. Add a cc-core regression test for the edge-once emission and the error_type classifier.
- **Skeptic check:** ADAPT holds; I could not refute it. Both default attacks fail against the codebase. (a) "Proxy feature in disguise" — only partly true, and the judge already neutralized it. The LiteLLM ErrorLogs schema (status_code, api_base, model_group, request_kwargs, request_id) IS proxy-native and unobservable to Shepherd, which makes NO API calls. That kills a verbatim ADD, which the judge correctly rejected. But the ADAPT is built solely on the one thing Shepherd genuinely observes LOCALLY: the {type=system, subtype=api_error} transcript line + its error.message, already parsed by core.transcriptError (cc-core.lua:1588, the api_error branch at 1600-1603). That is a real local filesystem signal, not a proxy artifact, so the stripped-down feature is not a disguised proxy capability. (b) "Already have it" — does NOT hold; verified directly. The error is DETECTED (status flips to "error" at claude-dashboard.lua:5425-5428) but NO ledger event is emitted at that edge. The two API-error-adjacent events that DO exist — `continue` (manual resume, line 2437) and `auto_continue` (opt-in, line 5615) — fire only on the RECOVERY action; if the user neither clicks Continue nor enables Auto-Continue (off by default), an errored turn leaves zero ledger trace. Confirmed there is no "error" event type anywhere: absent from NARRATE (cc-core.lua:640-667), EV_EMOJI (dashboard 4632), the filter dropdown (3313-3332), and fleetStats (cc-core.lua:954-1038 has no error/failed-turn bucket). So the ledger and shift report literally cannot answer "which sessions errored and with what message." The judge's "records the consequences, not the event" framing is accurate. The Shepherd mapping is feasible and reuses existing primitives: the transition-edge guard via prev[it.key].status (pv read at 5386, written at 5619) is right at the detection edge (5425-5428); ledgerFor, fleetStats, the a-f-type filter, and the cc-core test harness (tests/core.test.lua already covers transcriptError at 297-329 and fleetStats at 1676+) all exist, so adding an error_type classifier + edge-once emission + a failedTurns bucket is cheap and in-pattern. Fits hard scope: local, single-user, no network, opt-in ledger; aligns with the stated reliability interest. Honest caveat (why ADAPT, not ADD): this is genuinely an EXTENSION of existing machinery (transcriptError + ledger + fleetStats), reshaped from the proxy schema down to the single observable field — which is exactly the ADAPT definition, not a downgrade trigger. Relevant files: /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/cc-core.lua (1588 transcriptError, 954 fleetStats, 640 NARRATE), /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/claude-dashboard.lua (5425 detection edge, 5386/5619 prev map, 2437 continue, 5615 auto_continue, 3313/4632 filter+emoji), /Users/adam/Programming/claude-instance-manager/tests/core.test.lua.

#### Prompt/response logging redaction + store-bodies-vs-metadata toggle  `[H/S]`
- **For:** Shepherd's opt-in JSONL ledger already records a prompt snippet for every turn (cc-status.sh userpromptsubmit -> {type:"prompt", prompt:$p}, capped at 200 chars). That snippet is logged RAW — a pasted API key, password, or PII in a prompt lands verbatim on disk under ~/.claude/cc-ledger, and the only remedy today is the MANUAL, after-the-fact redactLedger that can only rewrite PAST days (today's hot file is off-limits until UTC midnight). LiteLLM's core insight — scrub content/secrets BEFORE the payload reaches any log sink, so sensitive material is never written in the first place — is a genuine gap. A write-time toggle ("log prompts as metadata only" and/or "scrub secret-shaped tokens from the snippet") plugs the window where redactLedger can't reach and matches the user's privacy-minded, off-by-default posture. The metadata-vs-content control is also a legitimate dial: a user may want decision/tool/spawn governance events without ever persisting prompt text.
- **Against:** Half this feature is pure proxy plumbing with no Shepherd analogue: Shepherd makes no API calls and never sees a model RESPONSE body, so LiteLLM_SpendLogs.messages/response columns and "store full request/response bodies for replay" have nothing to store — there is no payload in transit to gate. The "metadata only" side is essentially already Shepherd's only mode: it logs cost/governance events plus a 200-char prompt snippet, never full bodies, and the shift report code even comments that it carries "no prompt bodies… nothing to redact." Shepherd also already owns redact + purge + a filter/export overlay and lets you pick which event types get logged at all (you can simply not log "prompt"). So the store-bodies toggle is a non-starter, and a large slice of the redaction story is redundant with primitives that already exist — risking scope creep onto a feature that's mostly already present.
- **Ruling:** Split the feature in two. The store-bodies-vs-metadata toggle is DONT_ADD on its own merits: Shepherd is not a proxy, never sees response bodies, and already persists metadata-plus-snippet only — the "store full bodies" mode would violate scope and the "metadata only" mode already IS the design. But the redaction-before-sink need is real and currently unmet: the prompt snippet is the one place raw user content (and potentially secrets) hits disk, and the existing redact primitive is reactive and locked out of today's file. The honest ruling is ADAPT — not a faithful port, but extend the ledger Shepherd already has with an optional WRITE-TIME redaction pass: a toggle to log prompts as metadata-only (drop the snippet, keeping the type:"prompt" event) and/or a lightweight secret-shaped-token scrubber applied in the hook before append. That reuses the existing opt-in ledger, settings UI, and redact vocabulary, fills the exact gap redactLedger can't, and stays inside the local single-user model. It is the redaction half only; the store-bodies half is explicitly out.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend the existing audit ledger. Add an opt-in setting alongside the current s-ledger-types control (claude-dashboard.lua ledger settings block / cc-config) for "prompt logging: metadata-only | snippet | scrubbed-snippet". Apply it at write time in cc-status.sh where the prompt event is built (the cut -c1-200 + cc_ledger_append { type:"prompt", prompt:$p } path) and/or in cc-lib.sh's append helper: metadata-only drops the prompt field, scrubbed runs a secret-shaped-token regex replace -> "redacted" before append. Keep the pure redaction-decision logic in cc-core.lua next to redactLedger/ledgerDayIsPast so it stays unit-tested; this complements (does not replace) the existing manual past-day FX.redactLedger/audit-redact + purge. The store-full-bodies half maps nowhere (no proxy, no response in transit) and is dropped.
- **Skeptic check:** ADAPT holds after scrutiny. I verified the judge's claims against the code. The two common errors do not sink it: (a) the proxy-shaped half (store full response bodies) was already amputated by the judge as DONT_ADD — Shepherd never sees response bodies, nothing is in transit, so that half maps nowhere; what survives is purely local. (b) The "already have it" refutation is the strongest angle but fails: Shepherd's existing primitives cannot deliver what's recommended. Confirmed in code: cc-status.sh:266-267 is the ONE place raw user text hits disk (cut -c1-200 -> prompt:$p on the ledger). The existing redact (FX.redactLedger, claude-dashboard.lua:586) is reactive, manual, per-row, and is HARD-BLOCKED on today's hot file (handler at 2151-2160 refuses with "try after UTC midnight") — so a raw prompt snippet sits unscrubbed on disk for up to ~24h, a gap redactLedger structurally cannot fill. The existing captureTypes allow-list (cc-lib.sh:192) is all-or-nothing per event type: excluding "prompt" drops the WHOLE event, which would zero out turn counts in shift report/insights (cc-core.lua:978 increments on type=="prompt"). The judge's recommendation — a write-time toggle that drops the snippet while KEEPING the type:"prompt" event, plus an optional secret-shaped-token scrubber before append — is therefore a genuinely new, local capability, not a re-add, and reuses the existing opt-in ledger, the s-ledger settings block, and the redact vocabulary (the field-redact list at dashboard:4699 already names "prompt"). This is correctly a narrowed ADD->ADAPT (not a faithful port; store-bodies dropped). Caveat: real-world value is modest (prompts are user-typed 200-char instructions, ledger is off by default, a worried user can already drop prompt capture wholesale), so the judge's "high" confidence is mildly generous — but modest value plus a real, unmet, local, primitive-reusing gap does not justify flipping to DONT_ADD. Verdict stands.

#### Slack alerting with a typed alert taxonomy + outage/latency/digest/hang alerts  `[H/S]`
- **For:** A typed, allow-listed, per-type-routable alert taxonomy with an outbound push channel is genuinely useful for an unattended fleet console — the human running 10 Claude Code sessions across editors isn't staring at the panel, so pushing "approval waiting > 5m" or "session wedged" to a phone/Slack is exactly the away-from-keyboard value. And several LiteLLM concepts map cleanly: (a) the alert_types allow-list is the same idea as Shepherd's NOTIFY_TYPES set (cc-core.lua:786) and ledger.captureTypes; (b) per-type routing (alert_to_webhook_url) is a natural extension of the single escalation.pushTopic; (c) the daily top-5 worst-deployments digest is conceptually identical to Shepherd's already-shipped shift report / standup (buildShiftReport, cc-core.lua:1136), which already ranks worst projects by weighted escalations/hangs; (d) "hanging requests" is literally Shepherd's stuck-session watchdog (isHung/trackProgress, escalation.hung). Adding a Slack webhook as a sibling to the existing ntfy FX.push, plus a per-alert-type allow-list/route map over the events Shepherd ALREADY raises, is a small, on-brand observability upgrade.
- **Against:** The overwhelming majority of the 14 alert classes are intrinsic to a multi-tenant HTTP proxy and have NO referent in Shepherd: LLM exceptions, slow-response/llm_too_slow latency, failed spend tracking, DB exceptions, deployment cooldown, new/removed model, single- and multi-region outages, and fallback reports all presuppose that the app makes API calls, measures per-deployment response latency, tracks spend, talks to a database, and has a notion of "regions" and "model deployments." Shepherd makes ZERO API calls — it reads local status JSON written by Claude Code hooks; there is no deployment, no region, no DB, no latency to measure, no spend ledger. Outage classification (Minor/Major per deployment/region) and the per-deployment latency/failure accumulator are dead concepts here. On top of that, Shepherd ALREADY has: the outbound push primitive (FX.push → ntfy + FX.playSound, both off by default), a typed event taxonomy with an allow-list (NARRATE + NOTIFY_TYPES + ledger.captureTypes), a notification-history Alerts tab with an unseen badge, a daily-digest equivalent (shift report), and hang detection. So copying SlackAlerting as-is is mostly importing proxy plumbing for events that can never fire, and the parts that do map are largely already built.
- **Ruling:** Strip away the proxy-only event classes (LLM/DB exceptions, spend tracking/budget crossings, latency, deployment cooldown, model add/remove, region/single/multi outages, fallback reports — none have a referent in a local console that makes no API calls) and what remains is a thin, real improvement: (1) a Slack webhook as an alternative/additional delivery channel beside the existing ntfy FX.push, and (2) a per-alert-type allow-list + per-type route map layered over the events Shepherd ALREADY raises (escalation, hung, auto_respawn, auto_continue, queue_starved, non-human gate decisions). That is an ADAPT, not an ADD: the need (route specific alert classes to specific outbound channels) is real, but it must be reshaped onto Shepherd's existing NOTIFY_TYPES taxonomy and FX.push delivery rather than the SlackAlerting class. The digest and hang pieces are already shipped (shift report, stuck-session watchdog), so they're DONT_ADD-already-have-it within this candidate. It's not DONT_ADD overall because the genuine sliver — Slack channel + per-type allow-list/routing on existing events — does improve an unattended local fleet console and aligns with the away-from-keyboard use case.
- **How (Shepherd):** Add a Slack webhook sibling to FX.push (claude-dashboard.lua:973, alongside the ntfy POST) — same off-by-default pattern, webhook URL resolved via $ENV through the spawned shell, never stored. Generalize escalation.push/pushTopic into an alert routing block: reuse cc-core.lua NOTIFY_TYPES (:786) as the canonical alert-type set, add a config allow-list (which of escalation/hung/auto_respawn/auto_continue/queue_starved/non-human-decision to push) and an optional per-type channel map (type → ntfy topic OR slack webhook), keeping the dispatch in the refresh loop where escSound/escPush already fire (claude-dashboard.lua:5309-5313, ~5540). The daily-digest ask is already the shift report (buildShiftReport, cc-core.lua:1136) — optionally let it be pushed on a schedule; the hang alert is already the watchdog (escalation.hung). Do NOT build: outage/region classification, deployment latency (llm_too_slow), spend/DB/model-add-remove/fallback alerts — no referent in a non-proxy local console.
- **Skeptic check:** Refutation fails; the ADAPT survives. Checked both failure modes against the code. (a) Proxy-in-disguise: overwhelmingly true of the CANDIDATE — ~11 of the 14 event classes (LLM/DB exceptions, spend tracking, budget crossings, llm_too_slow latency, deployment cooldown, model add/remove, single/multi-region outages, fallback reports) have zero referent here (grep for litellm/deployment/outage/region/budget = no hits; Shepherd makes no API calls, has no deployments/regions/spend). The judge already stripped ALL of them — correctly. (b) Already-have-it: two of the three kept pieces ARE shipped — the daily digest = buildShiftReport (cc-core.lua:1136, STANDUP_PROBLEM=escalation/hung/queue_starved), the hang alert = isHung watchdog (cc-core.lua:2375, ledger type 'hung'); the judge flagged both as DONT_ADD-already-have. What genuinely survives is a thin LOCAL improvement that is NOT present: (1) a Slack webhook sibling to FX.push, which today is ntfy.sh-only (claude-dashboard.lua:973-979); and (2) a per-alert-type allow-list + per-type channel route map over events Shepherd already raises (NOTIFY_TYPES, cc-core.lua:786). Verified that today escalation.push/escalation.pushTopic is a SINGLE global ntfy topic gating BOTH escalation and hung to one destination (lines 5312-5313, 5541-5544, 5561) — there is no allow-list and no per-type routing. So the sliver is real, local, single-user, off-by-default-compatible, no network listener (an outbound webhook POST mirrors the existing ntfy POST), no secrets (URL via $ENV). It is correctly NOT ADD (discard the SlackAlerting class + 11 proxy event classes) and NOT DONT_ADD (a genuine non-present local improvement remains). Judge's Shepherd mapping (FX.push sibling + generalize escalation routing using NOTIFY_TYPES, dispatch where escSound/escPush fire ~5540-5561) matches the actual code.

#### Dynamic per-request callback disabling  `[H/S]`
- **For:** The legitimate kernel is real even locally: "don't log THIS sensitive thing." Shepherd's ledger already records prompts (`prompt` events) and tool requests, so a sensitive project's prompt text can land in `~/.claude/cc-ledger/*.jsonl`. The current escape hatches are coarse (disable the whole ledger, or restrict `ledger.captureTypes` fleet-wide, or redact after the fact via the 📜 overlay). A finer, opt-in suppression scoped to a sensitive session/project — "don't ledger prompts for this cwd" — fills a genuine gap, and it lands almost for free: the suppression check belongs right next to the existing `cc_ledger_enabled` gate in `cc-lib.sh`/`cc-status.sh`, exactly where LiteLLM puts its check (before dispatching to a logging callback). It also dovetails with the user's saved-agent interest: a saved agent profile for a sensitive client could carry "no-ledger" as a property.
- **Against:** The feature as built is multi-tenant-proxy machinery. It exists because (a) the proxy fans out to many external sinks (Langfuse/Datadog/S3) and (b) there's a trust boundary — the request SENDER wants to override the OPERATOR's logging policy for one call, gated behind a premium tier and an admin toggle. Shepherd has none of that: one local JSONL sink, no network callers sending headers, no premium tier, and the operator IS the only user, so there's nobody to override. The per-request granularity is the wrong axis — Shepherd's log is already sliceable per event-type (`captureTypes`) and per-session, and after-the-fact per-row redaction already covers "remove the sensitive entry." Building even the adapted version is arguably redundant with three existing controls for a single-user console where the user can just leave the ledger off.
- **Ruling:** DONT_ADD was tempting (the header/body/premium/admin design is pure proxy plumbing, and three existing controls already approximate the goal), but the UNDERLYING need — selectively suppressing logging on sensitive calls without flipping global config — is real and maps cleanly onto a primitive Shepherd already owns. The deciding factor: LiteLLM's actual mechanism is "before dispatching to each logging callback, check a skip-list," and Shepherd's exact equivalent is the `cc_ledger_enabled` / `captureTypes` gate inside `cc_ledger_append` that every hook already passes through. Adding a per-session/per-cwd suppression there is a natural extension of an existing feature (ADAPT's definition), not net-new scope. What we explicitly DROP is everything multi-tenant: the request header, the body field, the premium gate, the admin/client split — none of it has meaning in a local single-user console. So: reshape to Shepherd's primitives, keep it tiny and opt-in, default off like the rest of the ledger config.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend the ledger-suppression check that already lives at the dispatch point. Add an optional `ledger.suppress` rule (e.g. a list of cwd globs or a per-session "no-ledger" flag) read by `cc_ledger_append()` in cc-lib.sh, right beside `cc_ledger_enabled()` and the existing `captureTypes` allow-list — when a session/cwd matches, skip the prompt/tool_request writes for that session. The match itself is pure logic in cc-core.lua (same shape as the existing autoAllow/autoDeny glob matching). Optionally surface a per-tile "pause logging" toggle in claude-dashboard.lua and let a future saved-agent profile carry the no-ledger property. NO header/body parsing, NO premium gate, NO admin toggle — those are proxy-only.
- **Skeptic check:** I tried to refute the ADAPT and could not. Both common error-traps were checked and neither fully applies. (a) "Proxy feature in disguise": The LiteLLM mechanism (x-litellm-disable-callbacks header / body field / premium gate / admin-vs-client split) IS pure multi-tenant HTTP plumbing — but the judge already strips ALL of it and keeps only the underlying primitive: "before dispatching to a logging callback, consult a skip-list." That primitive is local-meaningful and has nothing network-bound left once the header/body/premium/admin are dropped. So the trap is acknowledged and disarmed, not fallen into. (b) "Already have it": Verified against the real code. Shepherd's existing ledger controls are GLOBAL (`cc_ledger_enabled()` reads `ledger.enabled`; `captureTypes` is an event-TYPE allow-list — both at cc-lib.sh:181-196) and AFTER-THE-FACT (`redact`/`purge`, and `FX.redactLedger` only works on PAST days because today's file is hot with O_APPEND). I grepped for pause-log / opt-out / per-session / no-ledger / sensitive across all .lua/.sh and found NONE. So selectively suppressing the WRITE for a sensitive session/cwd, before it lands, is genuinely absent — it is neither a global flip nor an after-the-fact scrub. The judge's Shepherd mapping is accurate and the fit is unusually clean. The exact dispatch point exists: `cc_ledger_append()` (cc-lib.sh:187) already receives cwd, session_id and projectKey (cc-status.sh:258-261) and already short-circuits on a captureTypes allow-list right where a `ledger.suppress` cwd-glob / per-session no-ledger check would sit beside it. cwd-glob matching by shell `case` is trivial there and is the SAME shape as the existing autoAllow/autoDeny gate. The feature is opt-in, default-off, single-user, filesystem-only, no server — fully inside Shepherd's hard scope. It also dovetails with the user's stated future interest (a saved-agent profile could carry a no-ledger property). Two caveats that lower confidence slightly but do NOT change the verdict: (1) The judge says the match is "pure logic in cc-core.lua (same shape as autoAllow/autoDeny)." That is partly wrong — the autoAllow/autoDeny GLOB match actually lives in cc-approve.sh (shell `case "$pat" in`, lines 50-57); cc-core.lua only does risk-scoring/provenance over decisions. The match for this feature would live shell-side in cc_ledger_append, not in cc-core.lua. Minor mis-attribution, same trivial difficulty. (2) For completeness the suppression must be added at BOTH dispatch points that share the gate: cc_ledger_append (hook events) AND FX.appendLedger (claude-dashboard.lua:532, operator actions like spawn/relabel/redact) — otherwise a "suppressed" session's spawn/relabel still gets logged. Still small and parallel, since both points already carry the identical enabled+captureTypes gate. Verdict holds at ADAPT: reshape to Shepherd's primitives (a `ledger.suppress` rule — cwd globs and/or a per-session no-ledger flag — checked beside cc_ledger_enabled/captureTypes at both append points), keep it tiny and opt-in, default off. Drop the header, body field, premium gate, and admin/client split entirely as proxy-only. Not ADD (it's an extension of the existing ledger gate, not net-new scope) and not DONT_ADD (the prevent-the-write-for-sensitive-sessions capability is real and genuinely missing).

#### Adaptive-router per-turn quality signals (satisfaction/failure/loop/stagnation/misalignment/disengagement/exhaustion)  `[H/M]`
- **For:** The underlying need — "is this session quietly stuck, looping, or frustrating its user even though the status file still says working/done?" — is precisely Shepherd's status-derivation and observability job, and several of these signals are NEW information Shepherd doesn't yet surface. Crucially the infrastructure is already in place: refresh() already reads the last 64KB of each session's transcript (ACTIVITY_BYTES=65536 in claude-dashboard.lua:109), already JSON-decodes assistant/user turns (core.transcriptSnippet, cc-core.lua:1544), and already does exactly this kind of "scan the tail backwards and classify the latest significant event" detection for API errors (core.transcriptError, cc-core.lua:1588). So adding O(1)-per-turn pure detectors over that same bounded tail is a natural extension, not new plumbing. The user-side semantic signals are the real prize: misalignment (user rephrasing the same ask), disengagement ("just give me a human / forget it"), and satisfaction have no Shepherd equivalent today and would meaningfully enrich the risk score (cc-core.lua:1371), the standup/shift report (fleetStandup, cc-core.lua:1137), and a per-tile health glyph — telling the operator which of N working tiles actually deserves attention. The logic is pure and window-bounded, a perfect fit for the unit-tested cc-core.lua layer.
- **Against:** A large fraction of these signals are already covered, and the canonical implementation is proxy machinery. failure(tool error) and exhaustion(429/timeout/context-limit) duplicate core.transcriptError's frozen-on-API-error detection plus the Auto-Continue recovery path; loop(same tool repeated N times) and stagnation(near-duplicate replies) substantially overlap the stuck-session watchdog (core.isHung/trackProgress/applyProgress, cc-core.lua:2367) which already flags a working session making no transcript progress, and with stale detection. Adding parallel detectors risks two systems disagreeing about whether a tile is wedged. More damaging: the LiteLLM feature's reason for existing is to feed an ADAPTIVE ROUTER — counters persisted last-write-wins per (session, router, model) in LiteLLM_AdaptiveRouterSession (schema.prisma:1297-1324) so the proxy can re-rank model DEPLOYMENTS for the next request. Shepherd does not route between model deployments per turn and has no router to feed; that entire persistence/feedback half is multi-tenant-proxy scope. The semantic detectors are also fragile — regex sentiment on agent transcripts will misfire (false "disengagement"/"satisfaction"), and a noisy false health alarm in a keyboard-driven console erodes trust in the real escalation/hung alerts.
- **Ruling:** Not a clean ADD (the as-is feature is bound to a per-turn model-deployment router Shepherd doesn't have, and ~half the signals duplicate the watchdog/transcriptError/stale machinery) and not a DONT_ADD (the NEED is genuinely Shepherd's, and 2-3 signals are net-new). That is the textbook ADAPT case: keep the per-turn classify-the-tail idea, drop the router/(session,router,model) counter-persistence layer entirely. Reshape it to Shepherd's primitives — implement only the signals Shepherd lacks (misalignment, disengagement, satisfaction) as pure detectors alongside transcriptError, gate them behind "enough context" exactly as the spec says, and explicitly DEFER to the existing watchdog/error/stale signals rather than re-deriving loop/stagnation/exhaustion (route those through isHung and transcriptError so the two systems can't disagree). Surface the result as a per-tile health hint plus a ledgered event that the risk score and shift report consume, and keep it OFF BY DEFAULT like every other automated signal. The semantic-regex fragility is real, so scope to a small, conservative pattern set and make it advisory (an indicator, never an automated action), matching how sessionRisk is "indicator only; no enforcement."
- **How (Shepherd):** Add pure detectors to cc-core.lua next to transcriptSnippet/transcriptError (cc-core.lua:1544-1609), reusing the tail that refresh() already reads (claude-dashboard.lua:5409-5412, ACTIVITY_BYTES). Implement ONLY the net-new signals — misalignment (user rephrasing), disengagement ("talk to a human"), satisfaction — and for loop/stagnation/failure/exhaustion delegate to the existing watchdog (core.isHung/trackProgress, cc-core.lua:2367-2417) and core.transcriptError so detection isn't duplicated. Emit a new ledger event type (JSONL, opt-in) that feeds (a) the per-session risk score (M.RISK_WEIGHTS/sessionRisk, cc-core.lua:1347-1435) as an advisory input and (b) the shift report (fleetStandup STANDUP_PROBLEM set, cc-core.lua:1136-1190) as a new problem line. Render as a tile health glyph in the detail panel. Off by default, indicator-only, no enforcement. Explicitly DROP the LiteLLM (session,router,model) last-write-wins counter persistence (schema.prisma:1297-1324) — there is no per-turn model-deployment router in Shepherd to feed.
- **Skeptic check:** Refutation attempted on both warned failure modes; neither sticks. (a) "Proxy dressed up as local" — does NOT apply. The original feature's core is LiteLLM's per-deployment (session,router,model) counter persistence tied to an adaptive model-deployment router. I verified Shepherd has no such concept: the only "router" in the code is the QUEUE dispatcher routing work to sessions (fleetStandup `by=="router"`, cc-core.lua:1169), not requests to model deployments; the only LiteLLM mention is the gateway provider picker (env injection, no proxy, no key storage, cc-core.lua:2618-2628). The judge EXPLICITLY drops the whole counter-persistence layer (correct — nothing to feed), so this is a strip, not a disguise. (b) "Already has it" — applies to ~4 of 7 signals, and the judge correctly defers them rather than re-adding: loop -> core.isHung watchdog (cc-core.lua:2375), stagnation -> watchdog no-progress + transcriptSnippet, failure -> core.transcriptError api_error (cc-core.lua:1588), exhaustion -> transcriptError + context-fullness bar + auto-continue recovery. Routing these through the existing watchdog/transcriptError (so the two systems can't disagree) is exactly right and avoids the duplication. What survives as genuinely net-new: satisfaction, misalignment (user rephrasing), disengagement ("talk to a human") — all USER-SIDE intent signals. Confirmed by grep that nothing in cc-core classifies user sentiment/intent; the current tail logic touches `user` lines only to detect recovery (transcriptError line 1598), never to classify. These are local, single-user-relevant (a triage hint for a human shepherding a fleet), absent today, and plug cleanly into existing primitives the judge cited: opt-in JSONL ledger event -> sessionRisk advisory input (RISK_WEIGHTS, cc-core.lua:1347) + a new STANDUP_PROBLEM line (cc-core.lua:1136) + a detail-panel health glyph. The tail they need is already read each refresh (claude-dashboard.lua:5410, ACTIVITY_BYTES). This is the textbook ADAPT case: clear Shepherd NEED, half duplicative (deferred, not re-added), a thin net-new core reshaped onto sessions/ledger/risk/standup, off by default, indicator-only (matching sessionRisk "indicator only; no enforcement", cc-core.lua:1343). It is NOT a clean ADD (cannot copy as-is — router/counter layer is meaningless and 4 signals duplicate) and NOT DONT_ADD (the 3 user-intent detectors are real and absent). The conceded semantic-regex fragility is the only soft spot, and the advisory/conservative-pattern/off-by-default scoping is the correct mitigation. Ruling holds at ADAPT; confidence stays high ONLY if scoped ruthlessly to the three user-intent detectors and the four duplicative signals are delegated, not re-derived.

#### PagerDuty incident alerting (failure-rate & hang-rate thresholds over rolling windows)  `[H/M]`
- **For:** The underlying need is real and partly unmet. Shepherd already detects per-session stalls (the `escalation.hung` watchdog: a `working` tile with no transcript growth for N minutes fires once per episode via sound + ntfy.sh push + a `hung` ledger event) and per-session API-error frozen state. What it does NOT have is the LiteLLM feature's distinctive shape: a FLEET-WIDE rate trigger — "more than X errors/hangs across all sessions in Y seconds." A solo dev running 10+ Claude Code sessions could have a systemic problem (provider outage, bad MCP server, a base-URL gateway down) that manifests as many sessions flipping to `error`/`hung` at once; today that fires N independent per-session nags instead of one "the fleet is degrading" signal. Shepherd already owns every primitive needed to build the aggregate version locally: the ledger records `escalation`/`hung`/`auto_respawn`/`api_error` events, `fleetInsights` already aggregates events over a `sinceTs`/`untilTs` window, sparkline bucketing exists, and `FX.push`/`FX.playSound`/the 🔔 notification history are the ready-made alert sinks. A rolling-window count-crosses-threshold check is a small pure cc-core function on data Shepherd already collects.
- **Against:** PagerDuty the product is a flat scope mismatch. It is a team incident-management SaaS built around on-call rotations, schedules, and escalation policies for staffed services — concepts that have no meaning for a single human watching their own laptop. Shepherd's hard limits forbid exactly the integration LiteLLM ships: it would require storing a PagerDuty routing/integration key (NO secrets storage) and POSTing to PagerDuty's Events API (NO network calls beyond the deliberately-narrow opt-in ntfy push and the leaderboard). The human is already sitting in front of the console with all tiles visible and an audible/push nag per stall — there is no "on-call who isn't watching" to page. And LiteLLM's value comes from aggregating failures across a multi-tenant proxy serving many teams' traffic; a local fleet of a handful of GUI sessions rarely produces the event volume that makes a rate-over-window threshold more useful than the per-session flag Shepherd already shows on the tile.
- **Ruling:** Splitting the candidate into vendor vs. need decides it. The vendor (PagerDuty: integration keys, on-call schedules, network POST to a team SaaS) is DONT_ADD — it violates the no-secrets / no-network / single-user limits and serves a multi-team-proxy world Shepherd isn't in. But the NEED behind the failure/hang rolling-window logic is a genuine extension of a feature Shepherd already has: the per-session `escalation.hung` watchdog and the `escalation.push` channel. The one capability missing is the AGGREGATE rate trigger — a fleet-level "X errors or hangs within Y seconds → one alert" — which is a real and modest improvement (de-duplicates the storm of per-session nags during a systemic outage into a single 'fleet degrading' signal) and is buildable entirely on existing local primitives, routed through the existing ntfy push + ledger + 🔔 history rather than to PagerDuty. So: ADAPT — implement the rolling-window rate threshold against the ledger and fire it through the channels Shepherd already owns; drop PagerDuty itself. Anything more (the actual PagerDuty client) is scope creep.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend the existing escalation/watchdog system, not a new vendor client. (1) cc-core.lua: add a pure function (sibling to `fleetInsights`/`isHung`/`approvalStale`) that counts `error`/`api_error`/`hung` ledger events within a rolling window (sinceTs = now - windowSeconds) and returns whether the count crosses a configured threshold, with once-per-episode debounce mirroring the existing `watchdog.alerted`/`escalated` carry-forward pattern. (2) cc-config.example.json: add `escalation.fleet = { failureThreshold, failureWindowSeconds, hangThreshold, hangWindowSeconds, enabled:false }` under the existing `escalation` block (off by default, like its siblings). (3) claude-dashboard.lua refresh loop: after the per-tile escalation/watchdog block, evaluate the fleet rate over `ledgerEvents` and, when it crosses, fire ONE alert through the existing `FX.push(escTopic,…)` + `FX.playSound()` + a new `fleet_alert` ledger type wired into `NOTIFY_TYPES` and the 🔔 Alerts tab. No PagerDuty client, no integration key, no new network endpoint beyond the already-opt-in ntfy push.
- **Skeptic check:** Tried to refute; the ADAPT survives. I verified both common-error traps against the actual code and neither applies to the ADAPTED feature. (a) Proxy-dressed-as-local? The PagerDuty VENDOR (integration keys, on-call schedules, network POST to team SaaS) is the multi-team-proxy part — it violates Shepherd's no-secrets/no-network/single-user limits — and the judge already DROPPED it. What remains is purely local: count events in a rolling window, fire ONE alert through the already-opt-in ntfy push + ledger + 🔔 history. No new network endpoint, no key, no multi-tenant notion ("on-call" collapses to "the single user gets one push instead of N"). So the proxy trap does not apply to the adapted version. (b) Already have it? Confirmed Shepherd has only PER-SESSION/PER-PROJECT alerts: approvalStale (cc-core.lua:2362), isHung watchdog (2375), and queue_starved (dashboard 5660-5672), each firing via FX.push(escTopic,...)+FX.playSound()+ledgerFor (dashboard 5525-5568). grep for fleet_alert/aggregate/rolling-window-alert across cc-core.lua + claude-dashboard.lua found NOTHING. There is no fleet-level AGGREGATE rate trigger today, so this is a genuinely new, modest improvement (de-dupes a systemic-outage storm of per-session nags into one 'fleet degrading' signal), not a re-add. Buildability checks out: the rolling-window primitive already exists in spirit (usageInWindow at cc-core.lua:1709; filterLedger {sinceTs} at 523), the alert channels exist exactly where the judge points (push/sound at dashboard 5540-5562, NOTIFY_TYPES at cc-core.lua:786), refresh already has ledgerEvents in scope (dashboard 5343), and the escalation config block is the right home (cc-config.example.json:39-50, off-by-default like siblings). One honest caveat that increases scope slightly but does NOT refute: the judge's mapping says count 'error/api_error/hung' ledger events, but in fact ONLY 'hung' is emitted as a ledger event today — there is no error/api_error ledger TYPE (the error STATE is derived live from transcriptError at cc-core.lua:1588; no failure event is logged on entering error). So the hang-rate half works directly over existing 'hung' ledger events, while the failure-rate half needs a small addition (log a new failure event on the error-state edge, or count over the live tile list). That is an implementation detail within 'extend the escalation system,' not a vendor client. Verdict: ADAPT holds. Implement the local rolling-window rate threshold against existing primitives, route it through the channels Shepherd already owns, default off; drop PagerDuty itself. Anything beyond that (the actual PagerDuty client / integration key) is correctly DONT_ADD scope creep.

### Cost & Budget

#### Pre-call cost estimation (/spend/calculate)  `[H/S]`
- **For:** The underlying need — "how much is this fleet costing me in dollars?" — is real and currently unmet. Shepherd already does ALL the hard input-gathering: cc-core.lua sumUsage() parses local transcripts into a per-model token breakdown (input / output / cache_read / cache_creation) per session AND fleet-wide (byModel), at zero API cost, and surfaces it as raw token counts in the detail-panel usage modal (claude-dashboard.lua:4920) and the fleet footer. The ONLY thing missing to turn those token counts into dollars is exactly what /spend/calculate is: a static price-per-token table keyed by model, applied as a pure multiply. That is a tiny, deterministic, network-free addition that fits cc-core's "pure logic, unit-tested" ethos perfectly. Cost-per-model is also a genuinely useful steer for the provider picker and the spawn-from-saved-agent direction the user wants (e.g. "this profile runs on Opus — pricier"). The cache-aware buckets Shepherd already separates make the estimate notably more accurate than a naive token count.
- **Against:** LiteLLM's /spend/calculate is an HTTP endpoint whose whole reason to exist is that a caller wants a server-side cost number without an account or key — it is proxy/multi-tenant plumbing (a route, model_group_alias resolution, the same cost_calculator used for billing many users). Shepherd is a local single-user GUI with no server and nothing to bill. The "pre-call" half of the feature is meaningless here: Shepherd never makes API calls and never sees a request before Claude Code does, so it can only ever do post-hoc estimation from transcripts already on disk. Worse, dollar accuracy is a trap: Anthropic plan/subscription sessions are flat-rate (the tokens are NOT billed per-token at all), Shepherd's own code already flags gateway/base_url sessions as hitting a different unknown-priced provider (isAnthropicSession), and a hardcoded price table goes stale on every Anthropic price change — risking a confidently-wrong dollar figure on a console whose credibility rests on accurate status. The token-usage tracking Shepherd already ships arguably covers the real "am I burning a lot?" question without inviting a maintenance liability.
- **Ruling:** The endpoint itself (a keyless HTTP route, model_group_alias resolution, shared billing cost_calculator) is pure proxy plumbing and DONT_ADD. But the NEED behind it — converting tokens to dollars — is real and lands one tiny pure function away from what Shepherd already computes. This is the textbook ADAPT case: reshape to Shepherd's primitives. Drop the "pre-call" framing (Shepherd has no request to price ahead of time) and the endpoint; keep only a static priceByModel table and a pure costFromUsage(sumUsageResult, priceTable) helper in cc-core.lua, then render the dollar figure beside the token counts that the usage modal/footer already show. The against-side's accuracy concern is the real design constraint, not a kill: label it clearly as an estimate, suppress or asterisk it for flat-rate Anthropic-plan sessions (Shepherd already distinguishes these via isAnthropicSession) and for unknown-priced gateway/base_url sessions, and keep the price table hand-tunable in config like context.autoCompactFraction already is. That contains the staleness liability. It extends an existing feature (token-usage tracking) rather than adding a new surface, which is exactly what the ADAPT bar asks for.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend the existing token-usage feature. Add to cc-core.lua, next to sumUsage/usageInWindow (~line 1684-1718): (1) M.PRICE_BY_MODEL — a static {input, output, cacheRead, cacheCreate} $/token table keyed by model, overridable via config.pricing; (2) a pure M.costFromUsage(usageOrByModel, priceTable) that multiplies the buckets sumUsage already produces and returns {cost, byModel}, gated to skip/asterisk flat-rate plan sessions (reuse M.isAnthropicSession) and unknown-priced base_url gateway sessions. Render the dollar value in the detail-panel usage modal beside the per-model token rows (claude-dashboard.lua:4918-4922) and optionally in the fleet footer; feed a fleet rollup into the shift report. Unit-test the multiply in tests/core.test.lua. No new route, no network, no key.
- **Skeptic check:** Refutation attempted on both common-error fronts; the ADAPT label survives but the "high" confidence does not. (a) Proxy-in-disguise? The LiteLLM endpoint (keyless /spend/calculate route, model_group_alias resolution, shared cost_calculator) is pure proxy plumbing — but the judge already stripped ALL of it (no route, no network, no key, no "pre-call" framing). The residue is genuinely local: a static priceByModel table x the {input,output,cacheRead,cacheCreate} buckets sumUsage already produces (cc-core.lua:1684-1704), rendered beside the per-model token rows the detail-panel usage modal already draws (claude-dashboard.lua:4920-4922). So (a) does not kill it. (b) Already present? Confirmed NO dollar/cost/price/spend code anywhere (grep of *.lua/*.json/*.md is clean; cc-config.example.json has no pricing key). So it's not a re-add. WHERE THE JUDGE OVERREACHED: README.md:329 is a deliberate, documented owner decision — "usage is shown in tokens, not dollars (subscription cost is flat; gateway/local model pricing is unknown)." The feature reverses a stated stance, not fills a gap. Worse, the judge's own required gating guts the addressable surface: flat-rate Anthropic-plan sessions get suppressed/asterisked, and unknown-priced gateway/base_url sessions get suppressed — which is EXACTLY README's two reasons. What remains priceable is only native per-token-billed API-key sessions, and isAnthropicSession (cc-core.lua:1740) can't even tell a flat-rate plan login from a metered API key (both read as "anthropic"), so the gate can't cleanly isolate the one case worth pricing. That collapses fit to a sliver for a local subscription-plan console. Verdict holds as ADAPT (tiny pure testable helper, plausibly the "leverage X better" owner) but the confidence is refuted: this lands at the LOW/weak end of ADAPT — a deferred optional helper, not a confident "high" extension.

#### Provider/deployment/tag budget routing filter (exclude over-budget backends)  `[H/M]`
- **For:** The underlying need is real and on-brand for Shepherd's stated direction. Shepherd already parses every session's token counts locally for free (cc-core.lua:1611+ "ZERO-COST" usage helpers, per-tile context bar, 5h/7d plan-window approximations) and already has named provider profiles (cc-core.lua:2618+). A budget guardrail is a natural extension: multiply the token counters it already has by a per-provider price-per-token in the provider profile, accumulate spend per provider/per project over a rolling window, and surface "this provider is at/over its window budget." That is exactly the kind of cost guardrail a single human babysitting a fleet wants — it answers "am I about to run up a huge third-party-gateway bill?" The over-budget signal could also feed the spawn path (block/warn before launching another session on an over-budget provider) and the ledger (a budget-threshold event), reusing the gate/ledger/spawn primitives Shepherd already owns. It maps cleanly onto the high-interest "providers / multi-model" area without inventing new infrastructure.
- **Against:** As implemented, this is a multi-backend HTTP-proxy load-balancer primitive and almost nothing about it survives contact with Shepherd's architecture. It presumes (1) multiple interchangeable deployments behind one logical model so there is a pool to filter, (2) a shared Redis-synced rolling spend counter across a multi-instance fleet, (3) a pre-call async_filter_deployments hook that drops backends per request, and (4) actual dollar cost visibility from being the proxy that makes the calls. Shepherd is none of these: it never proxies or calls the API (no cost is ever observed, only locally-parsed token counts), a session is pinned to ONE provider for its lifetime (a base-URL change needs a relaunch — cc-core.lua:233), and its only "routing" (4c-E, cc-core.lua:1981+) feeds a queue to a free SESSION, not requests to model backends. There is no routing pool to filter and no per-request decision point. The /provider/budgets endpoint is a network API surface, explicitly out of scope (NO network server). Building the verbatim feature would mean inventing a proxy Shepherd deliberately is not.
- **Ruling:** DONT_ADD is wrong because the cost-guardrail NEED is genuine and lands squarely in Shepherd's "Cost & Budget" gap (it tracks tokens and context but never dollars) and in the favored providers/multi-model area. A verbatim ADD is impossible: the per-request deployment-pool filter, Redis spend sync, and dollar-cost visibility all require being the proxy Shepherd refuses to be, and sessions are provider-pinned with no routing pool to filter. So the real, in-scope version is an ADAPT: a local cost/budget tracker that derives spend from the token counts Shepherd already parses (× a config price-per-token on each provider profile), rolls it up per-provider/per-project over a window, surfaces an over-budget indicator on the tile/footer, and optionally gates the SPAWN path (warn/block launching another session on an over-budget provider) plus drops a ledger event at threshold. That reuses usage parsing + provider profiles + spawn + ledger and adds zero network/proxy surface. Drop the per-request filtering, the Redis sync, and the HTTP endpoint entirely.
- **How (Shepherd):** Build on the local usage helpers in cc-core.lua (~1611+: parseUsageLine/sumUsage/usageInWindow) — add a price-per-token field to provider profiles (cc-core.lua:2618+ providerById) and a pure rollup that accumulates derived spend per provider and per projectKey over a rolling window. Surface an over-budget flag on the tile context/footer area and as a ledger event (the existing JSONL ledger). Wire the over-budget signal into the spawn modal / spawnProviderKey path (cc-core.lua ~2648) as a warn-or-block guardrail, and optionally into respawn so it won't auto-relaunch on an over-budget provider. NO Redis, NO per-request deployment filter, NO /provider/budgets HTTP endpoint — show it in the panel webview instead.
- **Skeptic check:** The ADAPT verdict survives, but the judge's REASONING is partly refuted and the confidence should drop from high to low/medium. Refutations: (1) The judge's central premise — a documented "Cost & Budget gap" — does not exist. I grepped context.md, todos.md, docs/, and README.md: no such gap or roadmap item. The opposite is documented: README.md:329 is a deliberate design decision — "usage is shown in tokens, not dollars (subscription cost is flat; gateway/local model pricing is unknown)." The maintainer explicitly chose NOT to track dollars. (2) The dollar-denominated framing the judge proposes is wrong for Shepherd: for native Anthropic sessions the subscription is flat-rate, so per-provider dollar spend is meaningless, and Shepherd already surfaces the REAL constraint (5h/weekly plan-window % from the OAuth endpoint, README.md:320-329). A dollar budget would be a fabricated, less-accurate duplicate of an existing metric. For gateway sessions the price is "unknown" by Shepherd's own admission and would require hand-maintained per-model price tables (config drift Shepherd avoids), producing estimates, not billed cost. (3) The original LiteLLM feature's actual substance — per-request deployment-pool filtering, Redis spend sync, /provider/budgets HTTP endpoint — is pure multi-tenant proxy and correctly dropped; sessions are provider-pinned with no routing pool to filter. Why it nonetheless HOLDS as ADAPT (not DONT_ADD): a thin but genuine local sliver survives. Shepherd already has the spawn path (cc-core.lua:2648 spawnProviderKey), provider profiles (cc-core.lua:2632 providerById), the JSONL ledger, and a usage parser that already captures input/output/cache tokens (cc-core.lua:1647 parseUsageLine, 1684 sumUsage, 1709 usageInWindow). A purely-local TOKEN-based (not dollar) spawn-time guardrail — "you've burned M tokens / launched N sessions against provider X this rolling window, warn or block the next spawn, drop a ledger event" — fits these primitives and is NOT present today (the existing "budget" in the codebase is a respawn-attempt counter keyed by projectKey, not spend; context.md:51). That is a coherent local single-user feature with zero network/proxy surface. Caveat: this survivor is closer to an EXTENSION of the existing usage-tracking + spawn features than a genuinely new capability, and should be framed as token-based, not dollar-based. So: keep ADAPT, but downgrade the judge's confidence and discard the dollar/Cost-&-Budget-gap rationale.

#### Per-entity budget caps (max_budget) + reset cadence + soft-budget alerting + projected-spend forecast  `[H/M]`
- **For:** The soft-budget alert + projected-spend forecaster are the strongest case: they map cleanly onto data Shepherd already collects. Shepherd already fetches the official Anthropic plan window (five_hour / seven_day / seven_day_sonnet utilization % plus resets_at) from the OAuth /usage endpoint, plus rolling local 5h/7d token totals. That IS the user's real "budget" — the thing that, when exceeded, actually stops their work (a rate-limit lockout) the way BudgetExceededError stops a key. Today Shepherd shows those bars passively (ok/warn/full color bands at 75/90%) but never proactively WARNS you before you hit the wall, and never tells you WHEN you'll hit it. A soft-threshold alert ("5h window crossed 85%") fired once-per-crossing and a forecaster ("at this burn rate the weekly window tops out ~16:40, before its reset") would turn a passive gauge into the single most useful thing a fleet console can do: stop the human burning their whole weekly quota by lunch across five parallel sessions. The plumbing already exists — usageBarLevel for thresholds, the w.alerted re-arm-on-reset de-dup pattern (exactly LiteLLM's soft_budget_cooldown), notification history, and the escalation/hung watchdog dispatcher — so this is reuse, not new infrastructure. The user also explicitly cares about leveraging the fleet better, and quota burn-rate awareness is squarely a fleet-management concern.
- **Against:** Almost the entire feature is multi-tenant-proxy machinery with no home in a single-user local console. The defining shape — max_budget attachable to a key / user / team / org / end-user / tag — presupposes entities Shepherd does not have and by hard scope limit never will: there are no virtual keys, no users, no teams, no orgs, no tags, because there is exactly one user and no proxy issuing credentials. The hard dollar ceiling (BudgetExceededError) makes no sense either: Shepherd never makes a billed API call, holds no secrets, and cannot meter or block dollar spend — token counts are parsed read-only from local transcripts at zero cost, and the actual paid metering lives on Anthropic's servers (Claude Code subscription/plan), not in Shepherd. The reset-cron (budget_reset_at rolled forward by duration_in_seconds via a background job) is also redundant: the plan windows already reset server-side and Shepherd just reads resets_at, so there is nothing for a local cron to reset. Building dollar budgets would require Shepherd to model pricing, accumulate spend it can't authoritatively see, and pretend to enforce a ceiling it has no mechanism to enforce — all scope creep toward the proxy it explicitly is not.
- **Ruling:** Strip the feature to its parts and only two survive Shepherd's scope. The multi-entity attachment model, the hard dollar BudgetExceededError ceiling, and the reset cron are all proxy/multi-tenant/server-metering concepts that DONT_ADD on their own. But the underlying NEED behind soft_budget + the projected-spend forecaster is real and currently unmet: Shepherd shows plan-window utilization but never proactively warns before lockout and never projects time-to-exhaustion. That need attaches directly to data and primitives Shepherd already owns, so the verdict is ADAPT, not DONT_ADD (the need is real but must be reshaped) and not ADD (the as-is dollar/key/cron form is pure proxy plumbing). The reshape: the "budget" becomes plan-window utilization (and optionally the local rolling 5h/7d token total for gateway sessions), the "soft_budget threshold" becomes a configurable utilization line (default off, per Shepherd convention), "soft_budget_cooldown" becomes the existing alerted-re-armed-on-reset de-dup keyed to resets_at, and the "projected-spend forecaster" becomes a burn-rate ETA computed from the rolling window slope against resets_at. No dollars, no keys, no cron.
- **How (Shepherd):** Builds on the existing usage system, not new infra. (1) Forecast helper in cc-core.lua next to usageInWindow/usageBarLevel (~1706-1735): pure function taking the rolling-window token series (or official utilization %) + resets_at, returning a projected crossing time / "will exceed before reset" boolean — unit-testable, zero hs.*. (2) Soft-threshold alert: reuse the usageBarLevel band idea with a config-set threshold (config key e.g. usage.softThreshold, OFF by default per Shepherd convention); when official five_hour/seven_day utilization or the projection crosses it, emit through the existing alert path. (3) De-dup: reuse the w.alerted re-arm-on-progress pattern (cc-core.lua:2404) but key the re-arm to the official resets_at boundary — that IS LiteLLM's soft_budget_cooldown / once-per-crossing, and the server reset replaces the cron. (4) Surface: a NOTIFY_TYPES entry (cc-core.lua:786, e.g. usage_soft) so it lands in the 🔔 notification history; render the projection inline in renderUsageFoot beside the existing resetsIn countdown (claude-dashboard.lua:4882-4899). The fetch/state already exists (FX.fetchOfficialUsage / computeUsage, claude-dashboard.lua:331/435). Explicitly DROP: max_budget dollar ceilings, per-key/user/team/org/tag entities, BudgetExceededError enforcement, and the reset cron — out of scope.
- **Skeptic check:** Tried to refute; the ADAPT (high) ruling survives. I verified every load-bearing claim against the source. FAILURE MODE (a) — disguised proxy? Mostly TRUE of the original feature, and the judge correctly amputates it. The as-is feature is pure multi-tenant server-metering: max_budget dollar ceilings, per-key/user/team/org/end-user/tag entities, BudgetExceededError enforcement, and a budget_reset_at background cron. The judge EXPLICITLY DROPS all of that. What survives is only the abstract idea "warn before you hit the wall + project when you'll hit it." No dollars, no keys, no entities, no enforcement, no cron, no network metering. The data source is the single user's own plan-window utilization, already fetched locally (claude-dashboard.lua:435 FX.fetchOfficialUsage reads the user's own OAuth token, read-only, to api.anthropic.com — a pre-existing in-scope call). So the surviving sliver is genuinely local + single-user. Refutation on (a) fails. FAILURE MODE (b) — already present? FALSE. I grepped cc-core.lua, claude-dashboard.lua, cc-config.example.json, README, todos.md, context.md for soft_budget/softThreshold/forecast/projected/burn-rate/eta/exhaust/slope/usage-alert: zero hits other than unrelated 'burn the main thread' comments and an autoCompactFraction note. Shepherd renders utilization bars (Session 5h / Weekly / Weekly·Sonnet) and a 'resets in' countdown (renderUsageFoot, claude-dashboard.lua:4882-4899), and bands them via usageBarLevel (cc-core.lua:1730), but it NEVER proactively alerts on usage and NEVER projects time-to-exhaustion. The need is real and unmet. Refutation on (b) fails. PRIMITIVE MAPPING — all four cited anchors verified exact: - usageInWindow (cc-core.lua:1709) + usageBarLevel (1730): pure, hs-free home for a burn-rate/ETA helper. Confirmed. - Official usage with five_hour/seven_day utilization + resets_at: fetched and pushed to the webview; resets_at already in hand for the slope-vs-deadline calc. Confirmed. - NOTIFY_TYPES (cc-core.lua:786) + notification history: the alert surface; adding e.g. usage_soft is a one-line table addition. Confirmed. - w.alerted re-arm-on-progress de-dup (cc-core.lua:2404): the structural analog to soft_budget_cooldown — re-key the re-arm to the official resets_at boundary and the server reset replaces the dropped cron. Confirmed. WHY NOT DOWNGRADE: The one real tension is that the surviving sliver is thin — arguably just 'add a threshold + a slope to data you already have.' But that is exactly what ADAPT means: real unmet need, attaches to owned primitives, must be reshaped from the proxy form. It is not ADD (the as-is dollar/key/cron form is unusable proxy plumbing) and not DONT_ADD (proactive 'you'll hit your plan limit before reset' is precisely what a local fleet babysitter should surface; default-OFF per Shepherd convention keeps it in-keeping). The (high) confidence is justified by the clean 1:1 primitive mapping. Verdict holds: ADAPT.

#### Grouped spend report endpoint (time-range, grouped by team/tag/customer/api_key)  `[H/M]`
- **For:** A time-windowed, grouped usage report is genuinely useful for a fleet operator: "over the last shift, which projects/models consumed the most?" is the same question Shepherd's 📋 Shift report and fleet footer already answer for activity. Shepherd already has every primitive needed to extend toward this: M.sumUsage produces a per-model breakdown (cost-shaped: input/output/cacheRead/cacheCreate/total/real), M.usageInWindow does rolling-window sums, and M.fleetStandup already groups by project with sinceTs/untilTs filtering and emits a copyable narrative. Bolting a per-project x per-model token rollup over a start/end range onto the existing Shift report is a small, on-architecture addition (a new pure cc-core fn reading transcript usage by projectKey, rendered in the existing Shift tab). The "broken to per-key/per-model + input/output tokens + request counts" shape is exactly what an operator wants when deciding which sessions to compact or which models are burning the plan window.
- **Against:** The candidate's defining feature is DOLLAR spend, and Shepherd deliberately cannot and does not do dollars — README line 329 states plainly "usage is shown in tokens, not dollars (subscription cost is flat; gateway/local model pricing is unknown)." There is no pricing table, no per-model rate card, and maintaining one is real scope. The grouping dimensions are pure multi-tenant proxy concepts: team, customer, tag, and api_key have zero analog in a single-user local console — there are no teams, no customers, no API keys (Shepherd never stores keys; it resolves $ENV via the spawned shell). The endpoint is premium-gated billing infrastructure for a network proxy serving many tenants. Strip away dollars, teams, customers, tags, and keys and what remains — per-project, per-model token totals over a window — is something Shepherd substantially already reports (per-model via sumUsage; per-project activity via the Shift report). So the as-is feature is a hard scope mismatch, and the residual need is largely already covered.
- **Ruling:** Split the candidate cleanly: the dollar-billing + team/customer/tag/api_key axis is DONT_ADD — multi-tenant proxy plumbing, no pricing data, no tenants, and it contradicts the explicit tokens-not-dollars decision. But the residual NEED — a per-project, per-model TOKEN usage rollup over an explicit start/end window — is real and fits Shepherd's primitives almost exactly. Shepherd has the per-model breakdown (sumUsage.byModel), the windowed sum (usageInWindow), and the grouped-by-project time-ranged report shell (fleetStandup/standupMarkdown). What it lacks is the join: token usage aggregated by projectKey across the fleet over a chosen range, surfaced in the Shift report. That's an ADAPT, not an ADD (must be reshaped: projectKey replaces team/customer/api_key; tokens replace dollars) and not DONT_ADD (it extends two existing features rather than duplicating them — today's Shift report counts events/prompts/approvals per project but NOT tokens/model cost, and sumUsage is per-session not fleet-grouped). The dollar dimension stays out unless/until the user supplies a pricing config, which is out of scope today.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend the 📋 Shift report path. Add a pure cc-core fn (e.g. M.fleetUsageByProject(usageEventsByKey, opts{sinceTs,untilTs})) that buckets transcript usage events (M.parseUsageLine) per projectKey and folds each via the existing M.sumUsage for a per-model {input,output,cacheCreate,cacheRead,real} + a turn/request count, filtered to the window like M.fleetStandup. Wire its output into M.fleetStandup's byProject rollup (add tokens/byModel fields) and render via standupMarkdown (the existing Shift tab <pre> + Copy button). Reuse M.formatTokens for display and M.usageInWindow's window logic. Stay token-only — honor the README cc-core.lua:1611 "ZERO-COST tokens, not dollars" invariant; no pricing table, no team/customer/tag/api_key dimensions. The impure transcript reads stay in the dashboard FX layer (it already tails transcripts for the per-tile context bar via lastUsage), so no new I/O surface.
- **Skeptic check:** The ADAPT ruling survives both common-error tests against the actual code. (a) Proxy-dressed-as-local? The candidate's NATIVE form — dollar billing across team/customer/tag/api_key axes — IS multi-tenant proxy plumbing, and the judge already amputated it: dollars and the team/customer/api_key dimensions are explicitly ruled out-of-scope/DONT_ADD. I confirmed the local invariant the judge relies on: cc-core.lua:1611 "Token usage (local, ZERO-COST...)" and README.md:329 "usage is shown in tokens, not dollars (subscription cost is flat; gateway/local pricing is unknown)." There is no pricing data and no tenants. The residual is projectKey + tokens — projectKey is already a real Shepherd grouping (M.fleetStandup buckets byKey on e.projectKey, cc-core.lua:1147), not a tenant axis. So the surviving slice is genuinely local, not proxy. (b) Already has it? No — verified by reading the code. Shepherd's FX.computeUsage (claude-dashboard.lua:331-392) aggregates usage exactly two ways: perSession (keyed by session id, with byModel) and a single fleet total (with byModel). There is NO per-PROJECT token grouping and NO since/until windowed token rollup. The Shift report path (M.fleetStandup -> M.standupMarkdown) groups byProject over a sinceTs/untilTs window but counts ONLY prompts/allow/deny/auto-respawns/escalations/stalls (cc-core.lua:1232-1242) — zero tokens, zero byModel. A repo-wide grep for any fleet/per-project token aggregation returned nothing. So the specific join the candidate maps to (token usage by projectKey over a chosen window, surfaced in the report) does not exist. That makes ADAPT exactly right, not a downgrade: it's not ADD (no genuinely new capability — it recombines sumUsage's per-model breakdown, usageInWindow's window logic, and fleetStandup's byProject shell), it's not DONT_ADD (not a duplicate — today's report has no token/model dimension and computeUsage has no project dimension or window), and the candidate must be RESHAPED (projectKey replaces team/customer/api_key; tokens replace dollars; the dollar/tenant core is discarded). The judge's Shepherd mapping is faithful: every cited primitive (sumUsage.byModel, usageInWindow, fleetStandup byProject, standupMarkdown <pre>+Copy, formatTokens, the existing per-tile lastUsage transcript reads in the FX layer) is present and correctly characterized, and it adds no new I/O surface. Ruling holds.

#### Spend grouped by tag/label + tag management  `[H/M]`
- **For:** The underlying need — "attribute activity to a project/feature/cost-center regardless of which session produced it" — is real for Shepherd, and Shepherd already has the two halves: (1) session groups (cc-core.lua:341, literally commented "Session groups / tags"), an arbitrary operator-assigned label keyed by projectKey with group-scoped filter + bulk actions and CRUD; (2) the ledger rollup `fleetStandup` (cc-core.lua:1145) that already aggregates prompts/decisions/auto-actions per projectKey, plus daily time-series buckets/sparklines (cc-core.lua:1247) and the local token-usage aggregator `sumUsage`/`usageInWindow` (cc-core.lua:1684+). The one thing missing is a GROUP dimension on those rollups: today byProject only rolls up by projectKey, so an operator who cohorts sessions into "backend"/"client-X"/"refactor" groups can't see per-group activity or per-group token burn. Adding a `byGroup` axis to fleetStandup and a per-group token rollup (and optionally a per-group token/turn soft budget that surfaces as an indicator, mirroring the existing per-tile context bar) is a clean extension of features Shepherd already owns, and it directly serves the "saved agents / cohort work" direction the user cares about. It's pure cc-core aggregation over data already on disk — zero new I/O, zero network, fits the architecture.
- **Against:** LiteLLM's feature as specified is a multi-tenant billing/chargeback primitive: DailyTagSpend rolls up DOLLAR spend per tag so an org can attribute cost across many API keys/teams and bill it back, with tags carrying their own dollar BUDGETS that throttle requests. Shepherd is single-user, local, and explicitly does NOT track money: every "cost" reference in cc-core is the phrase "this costs zero tokens / no model cost" — there is no pricing table, no USD, no per-request cost log, and there cannot be a meaningful one because Shepherd never proxies the API call (it can't see per-request billed cost; it only parses token counts the editor already wrote to the transcript). request_tags-per-log, the LiteLLM_TagTable.budget_id throttle, and the /tag CRUD endpoints are all proxy-server plumbing for a networked, multi-key, multi-tenant deployment — exactly the scope Shepherd's hard limits rule out. Building "spend by tag" as-is would mean inventing a dollar-cost model Shepherd has deliberately avoided, for a chargeback use case (cost-center attribution) that a single human running their own sessions does not have.
- **Ruling:** The verdict is ADAPT, not ADD and not DONT_ADD. Not ADD because the feature's literal core — per-request dollar-spend logging, tag budgets that throttle, and tag CRUD endpoints — is multi-tenant proxy billing that violates Shepherd's no-network/single-user/no-proxy scope and depends on data (billed per-request cost) Shepherd structurally cannot observe. Not DONT_ADD because the abstract need is genuinely present and half-built: Shepherd's "groups" ARE tags (same concept, same CRUD, same projectKey keying), and the ledger already does per-project activity rollups and daily buckets. The transferable sliver is adding a GROUP/tag dimension to the existing aggregations: a `byGroup` axis in fleetStandup (alongside byProject), a per-group local-token rollup reusing sumUsage, and per-group daily activity via the existing bucket helper. "Spend" becomes the honest local proxy Shepherd already speaks — token usage and activity counts — never dollars. A per-group soft token/turn budget can surface as an indicator only (like the context bar and risk score), never as a request-blocking throttle. This is extending two features Shepherd already has, in pure cc-core, which is the textbook ADAPT.
- **How (Shepherd):** Reuse the existing groups primitive (cc-core.lua:341 applyGroups/setGroup/groupNames, persisted in cc-groups.json) as the "tag" — no new tag table or CRUD needed; group CRUD already exists. Extend the ledger rollup: add a `byGroup` axis to `M.fleetStandup`/`fleetStats` (cc-core.lua:1145) parallel to its current `byProject`, resolving each event's projectKey to its group via the groupsByKey map. Add a per-group token rollup by feeding the per-session usage events (M.parseUsageLine/M.sumUsage, cc-core.lua:1611+) keyed by group, and per-group daily activity via the existing time-series bucket helper (cc-core.lua:1247). Surface it as a new section in the 📋 Shift report (standupMarkdown) and/or the Fleet insights overlay. Any per-group budget is an indicator only (model it like the per-tile context bar / risk score), NOT a throttle — Shepherd never gates a request on spend. Strictly token/activity attribution; no USD, no pricing table, no per-request cost log.
- **Skeptic check:** Refutation attempted on both common-error axes; the ADAPT verdict survives. ERROR (a) "proxy dressed as local" — partially TRUE but already handled. The FEATURE's literal core IS multi-tenant proxy billing: per-request request_tags, dollar spend aggregated across API keys "regardless of key," tag-CRUD endpoints, and per-tag budgets. I confirmed Shepherd structurally cannot observe any of this: a full grep of cc-core.lua for cost/USD/dollar/price/billing/spend found ZERO money concept (the only "budget" hits are respawn-attempt counts at lines ~2131/2206, not dollars). Shepherd reads token counts off the LOCAL transcript JSONL (parseUsageLine, cc-core.lua:1647 — only input_tokens/output_tokens/cache tokens, no price), never a billed cost. The judge already excised the entire proxy core (USD, request-blocking budgets, tag CRUD endpoints). So this error does NOT pull the verdict down — the proxy part was already cut. ERROR (b) "already have it" — also partially TRUE but does not eliminate the increment. Tag CRUD is fully shipped as groups: applyGroups/setGroup/groupNames (cc-core.lua:350/377/362), persisted to cc-groups.json with FX.saveGroups + a wired save action (claude-dashboard.lua:62/517/2384) and a JS twin GROUP_NAMES (line 3633). So NO new tag table/CRUD is warranted — confirming this is not ADD. But the cohort-level VIEWS the FEATURE wants are genuinely absent: fleetStandup only rolls up byKey/byProject (cc-core.lua:1145-1192), there is no byGroup axis; sumUsage tracks per-session/fleet tokens but never per-cohort; bucketEvents (cc-core.lua:1321) is fleet-wide with no group filter. A group is a cohort of projectKeys (one level above project), so 'collapse byProject up to byGroup + per-group token rollup + per-group daily activity' is a real new view, not a re-skin. Net: stripping both errors leaves a genuine, LOCAL, single-user increment — token/activity attribution by an existing operator cohort, surfaced in the 📋 Shift report / Fleet insights, with any per-group budget as an indicator only (precedent: risk score cc-core.lua:1347, context bar), never a throttle. That is the textbook definition of ADAPT: extend two existing features (groups + ledger rollups) in pure cc-core, no new infrastructure, no network, no USD. Not ADD (no new primitive/CRUD), not DONT_ADD (the byGroup/per-cohort-token views do not exist today). Ruling holds at ADAPT. One caveat for implementers: the judge's line citations are approximate (e.g. applyGroups is :350 not :341, fleetStandup byKey is :1145 not the cited region) but every named function and the cc-groups.json primitive verifiably exist.

### Governance & Access

#### Admin audit log of management mutations (who changed what, before/after, redacted)  `[H/S]`
- **For:** Shepherd's own management mutations are partly an audit blind spot today. It already logs SESSION-scoped operator actions to the ledger with before/after deltas (mode_change/model_change/effort_change carry from→to at claude-dashboard.lua:2431-2435; relabel:2375; spawn:1554; redact/purge:604/650). But the central management-config mutation path — the save-config handler at claude-dashboard.lua:1661-1687 — rewrites cc-config.json (gate.tools, policies.patterns.autoAllow/autoDeny, risk.weights, escalation, drain/respawn, provider profiles, launch-on-startup) and only print()s to the Hammerspoon console; it emits NO ledger event and keeps no before/after diff. So 'who loosened the autoDeny list, or armed autopilot, and when' is invisible in the audit trail. For a tool whose whole governance pitch is the ledger, having policy changes themselves be unaudited is a real gap. LiteLLM's exact concept — capture action + object + before_value/updated_values, mask secret-shaped values — maps cleanly onto a new family of ledger event types, and Shepherd already owns every reuse primitive: appendLedger (FX:532), the redact path, the cc-core SensitiveDataMasker analog isn't there but secrets aren't stored anyway, and the 📜 Audit overlay already renders typed events with an icon map and a type filter (3321-3329, 4630). Low marginal cost, closes a credibility hole, and supports the user's saved-agents/skills/MCP direction (those registries will be management objects whose edits should also be audited).
- **Against:** Most of LiteLLM's feature is multi-tenant-proxy machinery that is meaningless for a local single-user console and explicitly out of scope: changed_by actor identity, the api-key hash, the x-litellm-changed-by header override, deleted_by_api_key, dispatch to audit_log_callbacks, and a relational LiteLLM_AuditLog table over key/team/user/model rows. Shepherd has NO users, NO teams, NO keys, NO model deployments, NO API — there is exactly one actor (the human at the keyboard), so 'who' is a constant and 'before/after of a key row' has no referent. The secret-masking (SensitiveDataMasker) is solving a problem Shepherd designed away (it never stores secrets; it resolves $ENV via the spawned shell). And the storage layer is already built: append-only JSONL, opt-in, redact/purge/export/narrative. So adopting the feature as-described would be 90% scope mismatch plus 10% redundant-with-the-ledger. The truly novel slice — auditing config-file edits — is a handful of appendLedger calls, arguably small enough to fold into the existing 'roadmap: build on the ledger' rather than treated as importing this feature at all.
- **Ruling:** The underlying NEED is real and only partially met, which is the textbook ADAPT case — not ADD (the LiteLLM shape, DB tables / api-key-hash actor / changed-by header / callbacks, is pure multi-tenant-proxy plumbing that DONT_ADD scope rules exclude), and not DONT_ADD (Shepherd does NOT already audit its central management mutation: save-config writes policy/provider/gate changes with no ledger event and no before/after diff). Session-scoped operator actions are already audited with deltas; the gap is management/config-object mutations. The honest reshape is to reuse Shepherd's existing primitives: emit new ledger event types (config_change carrying a redacted key-path before/after diff, plus template_change/preset_change/profile_change for the editable registries) from the save-config handler and the other persist paths, with a single implicit actor (the local human, like the gate's 'human' provenance) instead of changed_by. Redaction reuses the existing redact tombstone path; rendering reuses the 📜 Audit overlay's typed-event narrative + type filter. This also future-proofs the user's saved-agents / skills / MCP registries, whose edits would be management objects worth auditing. Drop the API-key hash, x-litellm-changed-by header, deleted_by_api_key, and callback dispatch entirely — no referent in a single-user local app.
- **How (Shepherd):** Reuse the existing ledger, not a new store. (1) In the save-config handler (claude-dashboard.lua:1661-1687), before FX.writeFile(CONFIG_FILE,...), diff loadConfig() vs the overlaid cfg over a whitelist of governance-relevant key paths (gate.tools, gate enabled/GATE_FLAG, policies.patterns.autoAllow/autoDeny, policies.* enables, risk.weights/bands, escalation.*, drain/respawn/auto-continue, provider profiles, autoLaunch) and emit FX.appendLedger({type='config_change', changes={ {path, from, to} ... }, by='human'}). Add a pure cc-core helper diffConfig(old,new,paths) (unit-testable, zero hs.*) mirroring the existing pure ledger helpers. (2) Mask secret-shaped values in the diff (BASE_URL/key-looking strings in provider profiles) via a small pure masker in cc-core before the values reach the event — analog of SensitiveDataMasker, but Shepherd stores no keys so it's just defense-in-depth on profile fields. (3) Emit sibling events template_change/preset_change/profile_change from their save paths; same pattern. (4) Render in the existing 📜 Audit overlay: add config_change to the icon map (~claude-dashboard.lua:4630) and the type-filter <option> list (~3321-3329), and a detail formatter (~4641) summarizing 'gate.tools: A→B'. Redaction/purge/export already cover the new rows for free. Drop entirely: LiteLLM_AuditLog table, api-key hash, x-litellm-changed-by header, deleted_by_api_key, audit_log_callbacks (no users/keys/network).
- **Skeptic check:** Tried to refute on both common-error axes; the ADAPT ruling survives. (a) NOT a disguised multi-tenant proxy feature. The native LiteLLM shape — LiteLLM_AuditLog DB table, table_name/object_id/before_value/updated_values columns, api-key-hash actor, x-litellm-changed-by header override, deleted_by_api_key, and callback dispatch — all presuppose a networked proxy with users/teams/keys/remote callers.
