Shepherd has none of those referents (verified: zero changed_by / api-key-hash / x-litellm / audit-callback plumbing in claude-dashboard.lua or cc-core.lua). Added as-is this would be DONT_ADD, which is exactly why the verdict is ADAPT (drop the proxy mechanics), not ADD. The judge's reshape correctly strips all of it down to a single implicit by='human' actor. (b) NOT already present. Verified the central claim: the save-config handler (claude-dashboard.lua:1661-1687) calls FX.writeFile(CONFIG_FILE, ...) at line 1674 with NO FX.appendLedger emission. Grepping every ledger event type shows ~30 events that are all session-scoped operator actions (queue_edit, route_arm, gate_tools, mode/model/effort_change, nudge, clear, compact, respawn) or housekeeping (redact, purge, spawn). There is no config_change / template_change / preset_change / profile_change anywhere. So management/config-object mutations (gate config, policy autoAllow/autoDeny patterns, risk weights, provider profiles, and the preset/template registries persisted via FX.writePresets/FX.writeTemplates) are saved with no before/after record. This is a concrete gap, not a re-add. The honest reshape is genuinely faithful to existing primitives, all of which I confirmed exist: FX.appendLedger (532), the NARRATE table (cc-core 642) and EV_EMOJI map (4629) + type-filter <option> list (3313-3332) + evDesc formatter (4633) for rendering, the by='human' provenance convention already used for gate decisions (cc-core 792/933), and the redact/purge/export paths that cover new rows for free. A pure diffConfig(old,new,whitelist) helper fits the established cc-core 'pure, zero hs.*, unit-tested' pattern (alongside overlayConfig at 2498). It genuinely improves the local console: config saves can silently re-arm automation that is 'off by default,' and there is currently no tamper-evident record of when the gate/policy/provider config flips. Verdict ADAPT holds at high confidence; I could not downgrade it.

### Routing & Reliability

#### Static routing strategy selection (simple-shuffle / least-busy / latency / usage / cost)  `[H/S]`
- **For:** Shepherd ALREADY does deployment-within-a-group routing: project routing v1 (4c-E) feeds an armed project's queue to a free member, and `routePick` (cc-core.lua:2041) hardcodes ONE strategy — longest-free-first (smallest `since`, key-ascending tiebreak). That is conceptually identical to LiteLLM's model-group routing with a single fixed strategy. The candidate's core idea — let the operator PICK which strategy chooses the target instead of baking in one — is a clean, real extension of an existing, tested feature. Two of the five strategies map naturally onto local sessions: least-busy (Shepherd's current longest-free pick is essentially a least-recently-busy variant; a true "fewest in-flight" needs no API data since session status is already known) and simple-shuffle (random free member — trivial, deterministic-seedable, useful to avoid always hammering the same session/window). The introspect-and-render-options-in-the-UI pattern also matches how Shepherd's panel already surfaces armed toggles. Build is small: a strategy enum threaded into `routePick`, picker in the routing UI.
- **Against:** Three of the five strategies (lowest-latency, lowest-TPM/RPM "usage", lowest-cost) are pure multi-tenant-proxy plumbing that Shepherd structurally CANNOT implement: they depend on a CustomLogger logging per-request latency, token throughput, and per-deployment dollar cost into a DualCache as the proxy mediates API calls. Shepherd never proxies — it reads ~/.claude/cc-status/<id>.json off the local filesystem and makes zero API calls, so it has no per-request latency, no per-deployment TPM/RPM budget, and no cost ledger to score against. The whole "score healthy deployments by remaining rate-limit budget" model presupposes many deployments serving many users behind one key — exactly the network/multi-tenant scope Shepherd forbids. The Admin-UI-introspects-Router.__init__ machinery is also proxy-server scaffolding with no analog. And the residual real slice (selectable least-busy/shuffle for the next queued task) is arguably a marginal nicety: with a single operator and a usually-small fleet, the current longest-free heuristic is already sensible, so the value-over-effort of a strategy picker is modest.
- **Ruling:** The underlying NEED — choosing which member of a group serves the next unit of work — is a feature Shepherd ALREADY has (project routing v1 / `routePick`), so this can't be DONT_ADD "redundant"; it's a strategy-selection extension of a live primitive. But it equally can't be ADD as-is: the candidate is a 5-strategy metrics engine where 3 strategies (latency/usage/cost) require server-side per-request telemetry that a non-proxy local console cannot produce. That asymmetry is the textbook ADAPT case. The realistic Shepherd shape is: thread a small `strategy` field through `routePick`/`routeTask`, supporting (a) longest-free — today's behavior, the default; (b) least-busy — same data, no new telemetry; (c) shuffle — random among free members, deterministically seedable to preserve the keystroke-delivery contract and testability; and surface it as a per-project or global routing option in the panel next to the existing routing arm toggle. Explicitly DROP latency/usage/cost — they have no local data source and importing them would drag in proxy scope. So: real need, must be reshaped to Shepherd's queue/routing primitive and stripped down. ADAPT.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend project routing v1: add a `strategy` parameter to `M.routePick`/`M.routeTask` in cc-core.lua (~line 2041-2070), keeping current longest-free as the default to preserve behavior. Implement only the two strategies that need no new telemetry: least-busy (reuse session status/`since` already parsed) and simple-shuffle (random among `sessionFree` members; accept an injected seed/RNG via opts so it stays pure and unit-testable, matching cc-core's zero-side-effect contract). Persist the choice in the queue file alongside the existing `routing:true` arm flag (queueSetRouted shape, ~line 1997) so it inherits projectKey keying + respawn survival, or as a global queue.routing.strategy in cc-config. Surface it as a small picker beside the routing arm toggle in claude-dashboard.lua's panel, mirroring how other armed toggles render. Add core.test.lua cases mirroring the existing route-pick tests. Explicitly exclude latency/usage/cost — no local data source; would require proxying.
- **Skeptic check:** ADAPT holds. I tried to refute it on both traps and it survives, though the judge over-credited how much is salvageable. Trap (a) proxy-in-disguise: partly real but the judge already neutralized it. Verified at cc-status.sh:81/195 that the LOCAL status file carries only {session_id,name,cwd,status,updated,since}; grep for tpm/rpm/latency/cost/in_flight across cc-core.lua + claude-dashboard.lua returns nothing. The context-fullness bar (cc-core.lua:1747) is parsed from transcript usage for a display bar, NOT per-request latency or remaining-rate-limit data. So latency/usage/cost strategies have no local data source and are correctly DROPPED; importing them would drag in proxy scope. What remains runs purely off data Shepherd already has. Trap (b) already-have-it: Shepherd's routePick (cc-core.lua:2041) implements exactly ONE hardwired strategy, longest-free (smallest `since`, key tiebreak), with route-pick tests at core.test.lua:2348+. Making the strategy SELECTABLE is a genuine extension of a live primitive, not re-adding. So not DONT_ADD. Why not ADD: 3 of 5 strategies are dropped as proxy-only. And the judge's `least-busy` (fewest in-flight) is degenerate here: sessionFree is done-only (cc-core.lua:2023, only status=='done' is routable), so every candidate has zero in-flight work; least-busy either equals longest-free or is meaningless under the v1 done-only contract. That leaves essentially ONE net-new locally-meaningful strategy: simple-shuffle (random among sessionFree members, seeded via opts to stay pure/deterministic and preserve the keystroke-delivery + unit-test contract), threaded as a `strategy` field through routePick/routeTask and persisted beside the existing routing:true arm flag (queueSetRouted, cc-core.lua:1997) or as queue.routing.strategy in cc-config, surfaced beside the existing 'route' toggle (claude-dashboard.lua:3113/3138). So: real need on an existing primitive, must be reshaped and heavily stripped (drop latency/usage/cost; least-busy is degenerate, only shuffle is genuinely additive). That is the textbook ADAPT case. Verdict holds; if anything the scope is even smaller than the judge framed it.

#### Fallback model chains (per-group, generic '*', context-window, content-policy) + management  `[H/M]`
- **For:** Shepherd already owns the two halves this needs. Provider profiles (cc-core.lua:2618-2733: providerById/providerByModel/respawnSpec/providerEnv) bundle a model + non-secret env into a spawn and can reconstruct a session on a DIFFERENT provider; Auto-Respawn (stepAutoRespawn) and Auto-Continue (stepAutoContinue, gated by transcriptError detecting a frozen { type="system", subtype="api_error" } line, cc-core.lua:1588) already react to a failed session. Right now an error-frozen session is only re-typed "continue" or relaunched on the SAME profile — so a genuinely down backend (gateway 503, expired gateway auth, a model the endpoint stopped serving) just loops the same failure until the per-folder budget burns out. A fallback ORDER — "if this provider profile keeps erroring on relaunch, escalate to the next profile in a list" — is the missing reliability rung between auto-continue and giving up, and it slots cleanly onto respawnSpec (swap providerId to the next entry) without any new subsystem. The context-window-overflow case also has a real local analog: Shepherd already computes contextFractionFor / contextLimitFor per provider, so "this session blew its window, relaunch on a larger-window profile" is a natural, already-modeled escalation.
- **Against:** The feature as specified is request-path proxy machinery, and Shepherd is categorically NOT in the request path. LiteLLM intercepts a retryable EXCEPTION mid-request and re-issues the same call against the next model group, emitting fallback headers — Shepherd never makes the API call (Claude Code does) and only reads status JSON/transcript tails off the local filesystem after the fact (context.md:13). It cannot catch an exception, cannot retry a request, cannot emit a header, and cannot swap a model within a frozen turn — /model only works within the same backend; a base-URL change needs a full relaunch (cc-core.lua:163-166). The dedicated content_policy_fallbacks list is moderation-refusal routing that has no meaning for a single human watching their own Claude Code sessions. And the "+ management" half — CRUD /fallback endpoints persisting model->[backups] — is exactly the network-server, multi-tenant config-API plumbing the hard scope bans (NO network server, NO multi-user). Most of the literal feature is DONT_ADD; only a thin escalation idea survives, and even that risks scope creep onto autonomy loops that are deliberately bounded and off-by-default.
- **Ruling:** DONT_ADD the feature as written (per-request retry, generic '*' catch-all, content-policy routing, CRUD fallback API are all proxy/network/multi-user and structurally impossible for an out-of-band local reader). But the underlying NEED — when a session's backend keeps failing, fall back to a DIFFERENT model/provider instead of looping the same dead one — is real for Shepherd and extends two features it already has (provider profiles + auto-respawn/auto-continue). That is the textbook ADAPT case: the kernel reshapes onto Shepherd's spawn/respawn/provider primitives as a provider-fallback ORDER, while everything proxy-shaped is dropped. Verdict ADAPT, scoped tightly to: an ordered providers fallback list consulted only by the existing respawn path, charging the same per-folder budget, off by default, ledgered like the other autonomy loops. The context-window-overflow variant maps to "relaunch on a larger-window profile" using the already-present contextLimitFor; content-policy and the management API are explicitly out.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend provider profiles + auto-respawn. Add a pure cc-core helper next to respawnSpec/providerByModel (cc-core.lua:2659-2708), e.g. core.nextFallbackProvider(cfg, currentProfileId, reason) reading an optional ordered list (config providers[].fallbacks or a top-level spawn.fallbacks order). Wire it into stepAutoRespawn (cc-core.lua:2149) / stepAutoContinue (:2220): when an api_error tile (transcriptError, :1588) exhausts continue retries on its current profile, respawn via respawnSpec with providerId swapped to the next entry, charging the SAME per-folder respawnAttempts budget so it can't loop. Context-overflow escalation reuses contextLimitFor (:1765) to pick a larger-window profile. Off by default, ledgered as a new event type alongside auto_respawn/auto_continue (M.NOTIFY_TYPES :786). DROP entirely: the generic '*' catch-all, content_policy_fallbacks, fallback response headers, and the CRUD /fallback management endpoints (network/proxy plumbing, out of scope).
- **Skeptic check:** The ADAPT ruling survives refutation. I checked both default-error patterns against the code and neither applies to the surviving kernel. (a) Proxy-dressed-as-local? The ORIGINAL feature is pure proxy router (per-request retry per model-group, '*' catch-all, content_policy_fallbacks, fallback response headers, CRUD /fallback API served over HTTP to many tenants), and the judge correctly DROPs every one of those. What remains is not a proxy feature in disguise: 'when a session's backend keeps dying, respawn on the NEXT provider in an ordered list' rides Shepherd's existing local primitives only. respawnSpec (cc-core.lua:2688) already reconstructs a spawn via providerByModel (:2659) and FX.spawnSession; the dashboard already passes rs.providerId to FX.spawnSession at claude-dashboard.lua:5599 and charges the per-folder respawnAttempts budget (stepAutoRespawn :2149). A 'fallback' here is just choosing a different providerId for an already-existing process relaunch + env injection (providerEnv :2714) — zero HTTP, zero network, zero multi-user. The kernel is a config ordering over profiles Shepherd already spawns. (b) Already present? Verified NOT. Today auto-respawn faithfully rebuilds the SAME provider (providerByModel matches model+base_url to relaunch the identical endpoint), and auto-continue (stepAutoContinue :2220 -> handleAction continue at dashboard :5616) just retypes 'continue' on the SAME frozen backend — exactly the 'loop the same dead one' failure the kernel targets. No provider-switch-on-failure exists. The context-overflow variant is also new: contextLimitFor (:1765) currently feeds ONLY the display context-fullness bar and never triggers any relaunch (grep confirms no overflow-escalation path). Why ADAPT not ADD: the feature as written is structurally impossible for an out-of-band local reader (it's an HTTP router config). Only the reshaped kernel is addable — an ordered providers fallback ORDER consulted by the respawn path, same per-folder budget, off by default, ledgered as a new type alongside auto_respawn/auto_continue (NOTIFY_TYPES :786). The context-window->larger-window-profile escalation via contextLimitFor is plausible but thinner-value (Claude Code compacts rather than hard-failing on overflow). content_policy_fallbacks, the generic '*' catch-all, response headers, and the CRUD management API stay DROPPED. Fits the user's stated interest in reusable saved provider/agent profiles. Verdict holds: ADAPT.

#### Deployment cooldown after failures (allowed_fails / failure-rate threshold) + cooldown event callbacks  `[H/M]`
- **For:** The core instinct — "after something fails repeatedly, stop hammering it for a while and tell the human" — is genuinely real for Shepherd, and there's a concrete gap. Shepherd already has bounded auto-respawn (per-folder retry budget, latency-as-backoff) and bounded auto-continue (per-folder cap on API-error resume), but when those budgets EXHAUST on a crash-looping folder, the system just silently stops retrying. There is no explicit "this folder is in cooldown / quarantined, backing off until T" state — nothing surfaced to the operator, nothing the routing dispatcher can read to skip that project's member, and no first-class alert at the moment a folder gives up. LiteLLM's design supplies three transferable ideas: (1) a typed cooldown window with an explicit expiry (cooldown_time) rather than a budget that silently zeroes; (2) "exclude from rotation while cold," which maps cleanly onto Shepherd's project-routing dispatcher (M.sessionFree / queueRouted) — a quarantined member should not be fed queued work; and (3) an event at cooldown-entry, which Shepherd already has the rails for (the 🔔 notification history + escalation/queue_starved ledger types and NOTIFY_TYPES). This is an extension of features Shepherd already owns, in service of the operator actually noticing a folder that keeps dying.
- **Against:** The literal feature is load-balancer plumbing for a multi-tenant HTTP proxy and almost nothing transfers. It presupposes (a) multiple interchangeable backend deployments for one model so "take it out of rotation" even has meaning — Shepherd has one local Claude Code session per project, not a pool of fungible routes; (b) HTTP status codes (429/401/408/404/5xx) as the failure signal, which Shepherd structurally never sees because it does NOT proxy or make API calls — it reads idle/working/approval/done/error off local status files written by hooks; (c) per-minute success/failure rate accounting in a shared cache with min-volume gating, which is request-throughput thinking that doesn't exist at GUI-session granularity (a session does turns, not a stream of countable requests). The "failure-rate % over a sliding window with DEFAULT_FAILURE_THRESHOLD_PERCENT" math is meaningless when the unit is a human-paced agent session. And the observability callback half is explicitly Prometheus gauges / network alerting — exactly the multi-user, network, ops-dashboard surface Shepherd's hard scope limits exclude. Build the full thing as-is and you'd be importing a proxy's reliability model into a console that has no proxy.
- **Ruling:** DONT_ADD is too strong and ADD is plainly wrong (status-code-typed, per-minute-rate, rotation-pool, Prometheus — none of which Shepherd's no-proxy/no-network/single-session model can host). ADAPT wins because the feature, stripped to its primitive, is "explicit backoff state with an expiry + an alert when an auto-action gives up," and Shepherd has a real, narrow gap there: today auto-respawn and auto-continue budgets exhaust SILENTLY. The honest adaptation is small and rides existing rails: when stepAutoRespawn/stepAutoContinue hits its per-folder cap, mint a typed in-memory cooldown marker (expiry = now + cooldownSeconds, NOT a status-code rate), surface it as a tile badge, ledger a quarantine/cooldown event into the existing 🔔 feed, and have the project-routing dispatcher treat a cooled folder as not-free (sessionFree returns false) so queued work doesn't get dispatched into a dying loop. Crucially I reject the parts that don't fit: no status-code typing (Shepherd has no codes), no per-minute failure-rate %, no Prometheus/network callback (the "callback" becomes a local ledger/notification, which Shepherd already does), no multi-deployment rotation (the "rotation" is just the local routing queue). That keeps it inside the local single-user, off-by-default, hooks-driven model and makes a real operator-visible improvement, rather than copying a load balancer. Effort is modest because the budget bookkeeping, ledger types, notification history, and the routing free-member gate all already exist — this adds an expiry timestamp, a badge, one ledger event, and a guard in the dispatcher.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extends the existing bounded auto-recovery + routing in cc-core.lua. (1) In M.stepAutoRespawn (cc-core.lua:2149) and M.stepAutoContinue (cc-core.lua:2220): when the per-folder attempt count reaches maxRetries/maxAttempts, write a cooldown marker keyed by projectKey/cwd with expiry = now + cooldownSeconds (in-memory, same place attempts live; cleared on the sustained-health reset that already zeroes the budget). (2) Surface it as a tile badge alongside the existing drainingBadge (cc-core.lua:2100) and add it to NOTIFY_TYPES (cc-core.lua:786) with a new EVENT_META entry near auto_respawn/drain_close (cc-core.lua:661-665) so cooldown-entry shows in the 🔔 notification history + standup problems bucket (STANDUP_PROBLEM, cc-core.lua:1136). (3) Make the routing dispatcher honor it: M.sessionFree / M.firstFree (cc-core.lua:2018-2047) return false for a cooled member so queueRouted feeds (cc-core.lua:1994) skip it, mirroring "out of rotation." Config under the existing respawn.* / autoContinue.* trees (e.g. respawn.auto.cooldownSeconds, autoContinue.cooldownSeconds), off/0 by default. The Prometheus/alert callback maps to the local ledger append + notification only — no network emitter.
- **Skeptic check:** ADAPT survives scrutiny, though the judge oversold its substance ("high" is too strong). Verified against the actual code: (1) NOT a proxy-in-disguise — the LiteLLM essence (HTTP status-code typing, per-minute failure-rate %, multi-deployment rotation pool, Prometheus callback) all correctly stripped, and the residue is genuinely local. (2) NOT already present — grep confirms no cooldown/quarantine concept exists; the only "quarantine"-adjacent thing is the risk score, which is explicitly "never quarantines, indicator only" (cc-core.lua:1370). (3) The silent-gap is REAL: in claude-dashboard.lua:5592-5617, stepAutoRespawn/stepAutoContinue only ledger+print on the SUCCESS branch (step.spawn / cstep.fire) and on the not-respawnable branch; when wouldFire/fire is false because attempts hit maxRetries/maxAttempts (cc-core.lua:2131, 2206), nothing is emitted — auto-recovery exhausts SILENTLY. So an exhaustion notification is a genuine, useful, local improvement.\n\nBUT two of the judge's three adaptation pieces are illusory and inflate the verdict: (a) the "dispatcher guard" (make sessionFree return false for a cooled folder) is REDUNDANT — an exhausted-respawn folder is by definition frozen mid-`working` and stale, and an exhausted-continue folder is status==error; sessionFree already requires status=="done" AND not stale (cc-core.lua:2023-2024), so neither ever presents as a routing target. There is no scenario where queued work gets fed into a dying loop. (b) The "cooldown expiry timestamp / return to rotation" adds little — a respawned session is a fresh tile and the budget already resets on sustained health (cc-core.lua:2161-2164); there is no rotation pool to re-admit into. What actually survives is thin: one NOTIFY_TYPES key + one EVENT_META entry (near cc-core.lua:661) + a ledger emit in the existing exhaustion else-branch + a tile badge alongside drainingBadge (cc-core.lua:2100). That's a small notification add riding the existing ledger/🔔 system, not a cooldown subsystem. It stays inside the local, single-user, off-by-default, hooks-driven model and fixes a real operator-visible silence — so ADAPT holds, but as a minimal exhaustion-alert, not the cooldown-marker/dispatcher-guard apparatus the judge described.

#### Tag-based routing (route by request tags to matching deployments)  `[H/M]`
- **For:** The underlying need is already on Shepherd's own roadmap. todos.md:200-201 lists "task→session affinity/tags" as the headline deferred follow-up to routing v1, with the exact blocker LiteLLM solves conceptually: "no existing tag source; needs a decision on where a task's tag comes from." LiteLLM's model — declare tags on the targets, attach tags to the work item, route only to targets whose tags match (any/all), with an optional default/catch-all — is a clean answer to that open design question. Shepherd already has every primitive needed to host it: session groups (cc-core.lua:341-385, projectKey-keyed cohorts like "backend"/"refactor") are a ready-made tag source on the session side; the project-keyed task queue and the level-triggered dispatcher (routePick/routeTask, cc-core.lua:2035-2070) already pick a free session for a pool of work. Today routing is hardwired to one dimension — projectKey — so a "frontend" task and a "backend" task in the same folder are indistinguishable. Tag-matching would let one armed pool feed only group-matching free sessions, turning routing v1's single-project load-balancer into a capability-aware dispatcher, which is a genuine increment in the same pure/tested/opt-in idiom (sessionFree gains a tag predicate; routePick filters members by tag intersection).
- **Against:** Most of LiteLLM's actual mechanism is proxy plumbing that does not exist in Shepherd. There is no "request" object — work items are queue lines (plain prompt strings), so there is no request-tag field, and certainly no HTTP headers, so the regex-header matching (is_valid_deployment_tag_regex) is meaningless here. "Deployments" are model endpoints serving many tenants; Shepherd's targets are local GUI sessions one human watches. A "default-tagged catch-all deployment" is a fleet-availability concept for a network proxy, not a single-user console. And Shepherd deliberately deferred this exact follow-up in the June 2026 batch (orchestrator-next.md, todos.md:196-205) precisely because routing v1 is done-only and adding speculative dimensions behind dead flags was declined — the team wanted a UX decision (queue-line syntax vs. session group vs. provider as the tag source) before writing code. So the LiteLLM feature as-specified is mostly inapplicable, and even the salvageable kernel is something Shepherd chose not to build yet on purpose.
- **Ruling:** DONT_ADD is wrong because the need is demonstrably real and self-identified (todos.md names it as the top routing follow-up). ADD is wrong because the literal feature — request tags, regex header matching, catch-all deployments — is multi-tenant-proxy machinery with no analogue in a local single-user console: there are no requests, no headers, no tenants. The honest verdict is ADAPT: keep the idea (route work only to targets whose declared tags match), drop the transport (no headers/regex/catch-all), and reshape it onto Shepherd's primitives. Concretely, reuse session groups as the session-side tag source and let a queue carry a tag (front-of-line syntax or a queue-file field), then teach the existing dispatcher to intersect them. This answers the deferred design question with the most Shepherd-native of the three candidate tag sources (session group, which already exists and is projectKey-stable) and stays inside the off-by-default, pure-logic-then-fx idiom. It is an extension of a feature Shepherd already has (project routing v1 / session groups), which is the textbook ADAPT case. Because it touches the gateway between queued work and keystroke delivery, it must keep the keystroke-delivery contract (skip when no group-matching free window) and remain double-opt-in like routing today.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend project routing v1 + session groups. Tag source = existing session groups (cc-core.lua applyGroups/groupNames/setGroup, projectKey-keyed in cc-groups.json). Work-item tag = a new optional field on the queue file or a queue-line prefix (parsed in queueSplitLines). Matching logic: add a tag predicate to M.sessionFree and a member-filter to M.routePick (cc-core.lua:2020-2057) so a routed pool feeds only free sessions whose group intersects the task tag; an untagged task keeps today's project-wide behavior (the natural "catch-all"). Governed by the existing queue.routing config (still double-opt-in, off by default); ledger the routed feed with by:"router" as it already does. No headers, no regex, no catch-all deployment concept. This is the build-out of the explicitly-deferred "task→session affinity/tags" item (todos.md:200-201, orchestrator-next.md:196-205) and needs the same one UX decision (group as tag source) before coding.
- **Skeptic check:** I tried to refute the ADAPT and could not on either common-error axis. (a) Not proxy-dressed-as-local: the literal LiteLLM feature (request tags, regex header matching, default-tagged catch-all deployments) is multi-tenant HTTP machinery — no requests/headers/tenants exist in a local Claude Code console. But the judge already stripped all of that and kept only the portable idea "feed work only to targets whose declared tags match," reshaped onto real Shepherd primitives. (b) Not already present: I confirmed routing is group-blind today. routePick/sessionFree/routeTask (cc-core.lua:2020-2070) key off queueKey=projectKey and never read it.group; the only consumers of it.group are the header filter chips (groupNames, 362) and group-scoped bulk actions. So intersecting a task tag with a session's group is genuinely new behavior, not a re-add — and it is the textbook ADAPT case the judge names: a build-out of project routing v1 / session groups. Verified the mapping is faithful to code: applyGroups/groupNames/setGroup are projectKey-keyed (350-385), the queue file already carries an arm flag for free via projectKey keying (queueRouted/queueSetRouted 1994-2001), the dispatcher feeds members sharing a queueKey and ledgers with by=\"router\" (claude-dashboard.lua:5631-5657), and it stays inside the double-opt-in queue.routing governance. The need is real and self-identified: task→session affinity/tags is the top-listed deferred routing follow-up (todos.md:200-201), deferred only pending the one UX decision (which of queue-line/session-group/provider is the tag source) — and ADAPT answers it with session-group, the most Shepherd-native of the three. Adapted version keeps the keystroke-delivery contract (skip when no group-matching free window) and the off-by-default idiom. ADD would be wrong (transport doesn't port); DONT_ADD would be wrong (real need, not already present). ADAPT holds.

#### Per-exception retry policy  `[M/M]`
- **For:** The underlying need is real and already half-built. Shepherd detects a session frozen on an API error (cc-core `transcriptError`, dashboard ~5425 overriding status to "error") and recovers it with a bounded Auto-Continue (`stepAutoContinue`/`shouldAutoContinue`, per-folder `maxAttempts`, default 3). Today that recovery is one-size-fits-all: every api_error gets the same budget and the same "continue" action. But `transcriptError` already returns the error's display text (`error.formatted`/`error.message`), so Shepherd is one substring-classify away from doing exactly what RetryPolicy does — match LiteLLM's intent: keep retrying transient classes (overloaded/529, rate-limit/429, ECONNRESET timeouts) generously, but stop fast or never on permanent ones. A content-policy or auth/invalid-request freeze will NEVER fix itself by typing "continue" — burning 3 attempts (and 3 ledgered auto-actions) on it is pure waste and noise. A per-class budget (e.g. transient 3x, content-policy 0x = leave for the human) makes the existing loop-guard smarter and more honest, and it stays inside Shepherd's primitives (the error string it already parses + the per-folder budget it already keeps).
- **Against:** Shepherd is not a proxy and makes no API calls, so it has no exception objects — no RateLimitError, TimeoutError, or BadRequest to key a policy off. All it has is a free-text error string scraped from a transcript line, and classifying by substring ("overloaded"/"rate limit"/"content") is brittle, version-dependent, and locale/wording-fragile in a way LiteLLM's typed exceptions are not. The actual recovery is also far weaker than a retry: it types "continue" into the SAME session, which only helps the connection-reset/transient case at all — a true BadRequest/Auth failure isn't something Shepherd would "retry" regardless of class because there's nothing to re-issue. The existing single bounded budget already prevents the runaway-loop harm the policy is meant to avoid, so the marginal benefit is just "don't waste 3 cheap, ledgered keystrokes on a doomed error" — a minor efficiency, not a capability gap. This risks importing proxy-shaped configuration surface (a per-error-class retry map) into a console where it doesn't naturally belong, for thin payoff.
- **Ruling:** DONT_ADD as-literally-spec'd (no exceptions, no proxy, no per-route retries), but the need maps cleanly onto a feature Shepherd already owns, which is the definition of ADAPT. The right shape is not a generic per-error-class retry map; it is a small classifier on the error text `transcriptError` already returns, feeding Auto-Continue's existing per-folder budget: transient classes (overloaded/rate-limit/connection-reset) keep the current bounded retry, while clearly-terminal classes (content-policy violation, auth/invalid-request) get a budget of 0 so Shepherd leaves the tile in "error" for the human instead of typing a useless "continue" and spamming the ledger. That is an honest, in-scope refinement of stepAutoContinue, not the multi-tenant version. I keep confidence at medium because the win is modest (the single budget already caps the harm) and substring classification is fragile — it must degrade safely to today's behavior (unknown class = current default budget) so a wording change in Claude Code never silently disables recovery.
- **How (Shepherd):** cc-core.lua: extend `transcriptError` (~1588) to also return a coarse `class` field by matching the error text (transient | content_policy | auth | unknown). Add an optional per-class budget lookup consulted inside `shouldAutoContinue`/`stepAutoContinue` (~2200-2239): `maxAttempts` becomes a default with per-class overrides (transient = default, terminal classes = 0 → never fire, leave for human; unknown → default so it degrades safely). Config under existing `autoContinue` in cc-config.example.json (~136): add an optional `classBudgets` map next to `maxAttempts`. The same `class` can decorate the "error" tile/ledger event so the operator sees WHY it won't auto-continue. No new effect path — reuses the keystroke + per-folder budget already wired.
- **Skeptic check:** I tried to refute the ADAPT ruling and could not — it holds. I verified every load-bearing claim against the actual code: cc-core.lua transcriptError (line 1588) returns { message } from the locally-read api_error transcript event (no network call); shouldAutoContinue/stepAutoContinue (lines 2200-2239) implement a per-folder maxAttempts budget exactly as described; the autoContinue config block exists at cc-config.example.json:136 with maxAttempts; and it.error_message already flows to the tile + ledger (claude-dashboard.lua:4351, 4954, 5615), so decorating it with a coarse `class` reuses an existing path. Checked against the two failure modes: (a) Proxy-dressed-as-local? No. The literal LiteLLM spec — per-route retries on HTTP error classes in a multi-caller proxy — IS the proxy version, and the judge already rejected it ('DONT_ADD as-literally-spec'd'). The adapted form makes zero API calls and serves one user. (b) Already has it? This is the real pressure point: Shepherd already owns the bounded per-folder budget that caps the harm. But the refinement adds one behavior the current code genuinely lacks — today Auto-Continue (when enabled) will fire up to maxAttempts 'continue' keystrokes at ANY error tile, including auth/invalid-key and content-policy refusals it can never fix, wasting keystrokes and spamming the ledger. A terminal-class budget of 0 stops that and leaves the tile for the human. That is a real in-scope extension of stepAutoContinue, not a re-add — which is precisely the definition of ADAPT, not ADD and not DONT_ADD. The judge's medium confidence is also right: the win is modest (the single budget already bounds the damage) and substring classification on Claude Code's error wording is fragile, so it must degrade safely (unknown class = current default budget) or a wording change could silently disable recovery. Verdict survives scrutiny: ADAPT (medium).

#### Pre-call rate-limit & context-window filtering of deployments  `[M/M]`
- **For:** The literal feature is multi-tenant proxy plumbing, but its underlying principle — "don't hand work to a backend that can't viably take it right now" — maps directly onto Shepherd's existing project-routing dispatcher (4c-E). Today that level-triggered dispatcher (cc-core.lua ~1981+, `shouldFeedProject`) picks ANY free session of an armed project and feeds it the queue without checking whether the chosen session is near a wall. Shepherd ALREADY computes the two signals LiteLLM filters on: per-session context fullness (`contextFractionFor`/`contextBand`, cc-core.lua:1806) and plan-window throughput (the 5h/weekly usage bars, `usageInWindow`). A pre-dispatch guard that skips a candidate whose context bar is in the critical band (b5/b6, near auto-compact) or whose 5h plan-window is near its cap is the exact Shepherd-native analogue of "filter out deployments that can't fit the request / are rate-limited" — and it prevents a real failure mode: routing a fresh batch into a session that's about to auto-compact or hit a usage wall, wasting the dispatch. The context-window-fit half in particular is nearly free: the denominator math already exists; the dispatcher just needs to read it.
- **Against:** As written this is pure HTTP load-balancer machinery for a network proxy serving many tenants. "Deployments," "TPM/RPM caps," "reject 429," and "score healthy_deployments before strategy" all presuppose Shepherd makes API calls and chooses among interchangeable model endpoints per request — it does neither. Shepherd never sees a request's token count before it's sent (Claude Code, not Shepherd, issues the call), so the "filter deployments whose window can't fit the request" check has no place to run; Shepherd only observes usage AFTER turns land in the transcript JSONL. Rate-limit enforcement is equally out of scope: the official plan window is enforced by Anthropic, Shepherd just displays the % — re-implementing TPM/RPM caps locally would be inventing a limiter Shepherd can't actually enforce. Even the ADAPT version is arguably redundant: the operator already SEES the context bar and usage footer per tile and can avoid arming a near-full session manually, and routing itself is double-opt-in and off by default, so the blast radius of "dumb" dispatch is small.
- **Ruling:** DONT_ADD on the literal feature: TPM/RPM enforcement, 429 rejection, and per-request deployment filtering are proxy load-balancer internals that assume Shepherd makes API calls and selects model endpoints — it does neither, and the hard scope bars the proxy role. But the user weights Routing & Reliability favorably and Shepherd has a real, shipped routing dispatcher (4c-E) plus the exact telemetry LiteLLM's check consumes, so the NEED is genuine and extends an existing feature rather than inventing a new subsystem. That lands it at ADAPT, not DONT_ADD: add a "viability guard" to the project-routing dispatcher so it skips a candidate session whose context fullness is in the critical band (about to auto-compact) or whose 5h plan-window usage is near the cap, preferring a fresher member of the same project. This is the local-session translation of "only route to viable backends." I keep confidence at medium because the simpler, lower-risk slice is the context-fit guard alone (the data is already there); the usage-cap half is softer since Shepherd can only approximate a limit it doesn't enforce, and the operator already has the bars visually — so part of this is arguably redundant with existing observability.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend the 4c-E project-routing dispatcher in cc-core.lua (the `shouldFeedProject`/dispatch-decision block ~1981-2059) with a pure `routeViable(session, cfg)` predicate that the dispatcher consults before picking a target. It reuses existing primitives: `M.contextFractionFor`/`M.contextBand` (cc-core.lua:1806-1824) to reject a candidate whose fullness is >= a configurable band (e.g. b5/b6, near `autoCompactFraction`), and optionally `M.usageInWindow`/`usageBarLevel` (1709-1730) for a soft plan-window-near-cap skip. Gate behind a new `queue.routing.viabilityGuard` config sub-key (default OFF, consistent with the double-opt-in routing model). When all candidates fail the guard, fall through to current behavior (or hold) rather than dropping work. Unit-test the predicate in tests/core.test.lua (zero hs.*). No fx/network changes; no new API-call surface.
- **Skeptic check:** I tried to refute the ADAPT and could not break it below ADAPT; it holds, with a tightened scope. LITERAL FEATURE = DONT_ADD (judge agreed): TPM/RPM caps, 429 rejection, and per-request deployment filtering are proxy load-balancer internals that presuppose Shepherd issues API calls and selects model endpoints. Shepherd does neither (hard scope: no network server, no proxy, never makes API calls; the "agent" is a local Claude Code GUI session, not an HTTP route/deployment). README confirms LiteLLM only appears as a backend a session can point at (a provider profile), not a role Shepherd plays. ADAPTATION VERIFIED IN CODE (not already present, not proxy-shaped): The judge's mapping checks out against the source. The real 4c-E dispatcher exists — sessionFree/routePick/routeTask (cc-core.lua:2020-2070) — and is the actual per-project dispatch decision. contextFractionFor/contextBand/autoCompactFraction exist (cc-core.lua:1795-1826). Crucially, it.context_frac is already computed for done/peekable candidates at claude-dashboard.lua:5417, BEFORE they are collected into routeGroups at 5493 — so the fullness data is genuinely on the item at dispatch time with zero new I/O and zero new API surface. grep found NO existing viability guard, so the "already-have-it" charge fails: the visible context bar is passive observability; a predicate that diverts routing away from a near-full session is a new routing DECISION, not the bar. The 4c-E follow-up list (todos.md:197-205) itemizes unbuilt routing refinements behind design calls and explicitly declines speculative dead flags — exactly matching the proposed default-OFF, double-opt-in framing, and confirming this dispatcher is the accepted extension point. WHERE I BIT HARDER THAN THE JUDGE (scope, not verdict): The feature has two halves of very different quality. (1) Context-fit guard = the strong, faithful local translation: the proxy refuses a deployment whose window can't hold the request; the analogue is "don't route fresh work into a done session about to auto-compact (b5/b6), prefer a fresher project member." Real degraded outcome avoided (a mid-task auto-compact), pure predicate, uses data already present, not present today. Legitimate ADAPT. (2) Usage-cap guard = the weak half and the place the (a) proxy-in-disguise error actually applies: Shepherd's 5h/weekly numbers are a local APPROXIMATION of a limit it cannot enforce (no API role), so "skip a candidate because plan usage is near cap" is the closest thing to dressing up TPM/RPM rejection in local clothing, and it is redundant with bars the operator already sees. The judge hedged it to "soft"; I'd go further and CUT it from the guard rather than de-emphasize it. NET: ship only the context-fit predicate (routeViable using contextFractionFor/contextBand vs autoCompactFraction band, gated behind queue.routing.viabilityGuard default OFF, fall through to current behavior when all candidates fail, unit-tested in tests/core.test.lua with zero hs.*). That is a scoping refinement, not a downgrade — the core ADAPT survives scrutiny: it genuinely improves the local single-user routing dispatcher, is not proxy-shaped, and is not already present.

#### On-demand model health check (GET /health) + per-mode probe types + admin-only field redaction  `[M/M]`
- **For:** Shepherd's gateway provider profiles point Claude Code at an arbitrary Anthropic-compatible base URL (a LiteLLM proxy for Gemini/OpenAI, or a local/remote REST server) via ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL. Today there is ZERO validation that such an endpoint is reachable or correctly configured before a session is spawned against it — a dead/misconfigured baseUrl just produces a magenta "error" tile after the fact, and the operator has to guess whether the fault is the prompt, auth, or the endpoint. The codebase already half-recognizes this: respawnSpec returns canRespawn=false with reason "unknown gateway provider", so the concept of "this provider can't be used right now" exists. A LiteLLM-style on-demand health probe, reshaped to "test this provider" — a single local reachability/credential check of the configured baseUrl, run on demand from the provider editor or folded into `make doctor` — would catch a broken gateway before it wastes a spawn, and directly serves the user's stated interest in well-managed, reusable agent/provider configurations.
- **Against:** The feature as built is server-ops plumbing for a multi-tenant proxy and trips multiple HARD scope limits at once: it is an HTTP endpoint (Shepherd has no network server), it actively makes real mock API calls to model deployments (Shepherd explicitly does NOT proxy or make API calls — it only reads status JSON off the local filesystem), and its headline second half is admin-vs-non-admin field redaction with a response header — pure multi-user RBAC that is meaningless for a single-user local console (there is no "non-admin caller"). The per-mode probe matrix (embedding/audio/image/rerank/realtime/responses/ocr) is irrelevant: Shepherd manages Claude Code chat sessions only. "Probe every configured deployment and aggregate healthy/unhealthy counts" presumes a fleet of model deployments the proxy owns and routes across — Shepherd owns no deployments. Even the adapted preflight is arguably redundant with the existing error-tile + Continue/auto-respawn recovery loop, which already surfaces and recovers from a bad endpoint, and a credential check would brush against the "no secrets" line (the auth token lives in an env var the spawned shell expands).
- **Ruling:** The feature itself is a textbook DONT_ADD: an HTTP /health endpoint that actively proxies mock requests across many model deployments with per-user RBAC redaction violates three of Shepherd's hard limits (no network server, no API calls/proxy, no multi-user). None of that copies in. What lifts it to ADAPT rather than DONT_ADD is a genuine, narrow latent need underneath it: Shepherd DOES let a user configure gateway providers that point Claude Code at an arbitrary base URL, and it currently does no preflight on those — a dead endpoint is only discovered as a post-hoc error tile, and respawnSpec already models "unprovisionable provider" as a first-class state. The honest reshape is small and on-primitive: an on-demand, local, single-shot "test provider" reachability check (curl the configured baseUrl from a spawned shell via the fx table, degrade gracefully if the tool is missing, surface pass/fail in the provider editor and/or `make doctor`), scoped to the one user's own configured gateways — NOT a server endpoint, NOT active model probing, NOT RBAC redaction, NOT multi-mode. The redaction/admin half is dropped entirely. Confidence is medium because the adapted value is modest — the existing error-tile + Continue/auto-respawn path already recovers from a bad endpoint, so this is a UX/diagnostics nicety (fail before spawn, not after) rather than a capability gap.
- **How (Shepherd):** Adapt ONLY as a local "test provider" preflight, dropping the server/probe/RBAC machinery entirely. Add a pure cc-core helper next to providerById/providerEnv (cc-core.lua ~2632-2735) that builds a reachability-check command for a gateway profile's baseUrl (mirroring how folderScanShellCommand quotes a command, and resolveBin's graceful degradation). Run the side effect through the fx table (like FX.spawnSession / the rsync mirror argv at cc-core.lua ~2834) and surface pass/fail in the provider editor in the Settings overlay (claude-dashboard.lua s-providers, ~3213-3216) with a "Test" button, and/or fold a one-shot check into `make doctor` (install.sh --tools-only). Reuse the existing "unknown gateway provider" reason vocabulary from respawnSpec. Anthropic-kind profiles (no baseUrl) need no probe. No secrets stored: the auth env var stays shell-expanded, same as providerEnv today.
- **Skeptic check:** I tried to refute the ADAPT and could not. The feature AS WRITTEN is pure DONT_ADD — an HTTP GET /health server that actively proxies mock requests across many model deployments, adapts probes per mode (chat/embedding/audio/...), and does admin-vs-non-admin RBAC field redaction. That violates three hard limits at once (no network server, no API/proxy, no multi-user), and none of it copies in. The judge already strips all of it: server gone, active model probing gone, per-mode probing gone, RBAC redaction gone. So failure-mode (a) "multi-tenant proxy dressed up as local" does NOT apply — the dress-up was explicitly removed, leaving only a single-user, single-shot reachability check of the user's OWN configured gateway baseUrl. Failure-mode (b) "already present" also does not apply: I grepped all source (cc-core.lua, claude-dashboard.lua, the .sh hooks, install.sh) for curl/wget/reachab/ping/preflight/http_code/connect_timeout — zero hits. Shepherd does NO preflight on a configured gateway endpoint today; a dead baseUrl is only discovered post-hoc via the Error tile + Continue/auto-respawn path. So this is a real, non-duplicate gap. The mapping is technically verified against live primitives: gateway profiles carry a baseUrl (provider editor s-providers at claude-dashboard.lua ~3215 + the p-baseurl field at ~3813); respawnSpec already models the unprovisionable case (cc-core.lua:2699 returns canRespawn=false, reason="unknown gateway provider"), giving the reshape a vocabulary to reuse; providerById/providerEnv sit at ~2632-2733; folderScanShellCommand (3474) shows the exact quote-a-shell-command pattern, and resolveBin + `make doctor` (install.sh --tools-only -> tooling_check) show the graceful-degradation/diagnostics surface to fold a check into. A local one-shot "Test provider" curl run through the fx table is on-primitive and single-user. Why ADAPT and not ADD: the value is genuinely modest (medium confidence is right) — the existing error-tile + auto-respawn already RECOVERS from a bad endpoint, so this is a "fail before spawn instead of after" UX/diagnostics nicety, not a capability gap; and it requires heavy reshaping (the entire server/probe/RBAC machinery is discarded). Why not DONT_ADD: it isn't a proxy, isn't multi-user, isn't a server, and isn't already present. The only graze on scope is that a reachability curl is the nearest Shepherd comes to a network call, but a one-shot diagnostic of the user's own endpoint is categorically different from proxying model traffic. The ADAPT verdict survives scrutiny.

### Prompt & Config Mgmt

#### File-based prompt definitions (dotprompt .prompt files) with input-schema validation & model/param overrides  `[H/M]`
- **For:** The CORE idea — named, reusable, git-versionable prompt definitions that bundle template text + a target model/params + a typed, validated input schema — maps cleanly onto two primitives Shepherd ALREADY has and the user's #1 stated future interest. Shepherd's cc-templates.json (M.templateList/templatePush/templateGet) stores named reusable task text but it's VERBATIM today: no variables, no schema. cc-presets.json (M.presetList/presetPush) already stores a named spawn bundle {folder, editor, permMode, provider}. Fusing those two into a "saved agent profile" (preset + parameterized template + required input fields) is almost exactly the user's request: "pre-configured agents we can hand work off to, saved into a directory." Two pieces of dotprompt transfer directly and as pure, testable cc-core.lua logic: (1) Handlebars-style variable interpolation in the saved task body, filled at spawn time from the New Session modal (e.g. "Fix bug in {{file}}"); (2) a typed input schema with optional-via-'?' fields, validated BEFORE spawn so you don't launch a half-filled task — a natural guardrail that fits Shepherd's "validate before side-effect" discipline. The model/param half partially exists (preset.provider injects ANTHROPIC_MODEL; spawn already carries permMode/effort), so dotprompt's "the definition supplies the model, with caller-override flags" is just enriching the preset shape. Git-versionable .prompt files in a directory mirror Shepherd's existing operator-data-as-JSON convention.
- **Against:** Most of the LiteLLM IMPLEMENTATION is API-proxy plumbing with no analogue in Shepherd, which makes zero API calls and renders no messages: PromptManager's render-into-a-messages-array, DotpromptManager hooking completion via model='dotprompt/<model>' + prompt_id, compile_prompt returning template model/params, merge_messages folding template+client messages, and the ignore_prompt_manager_model/_optional_params flags all exist to shape an outbound /chat/completions request the proxy owns. Shepherd never owns the request — Claude Code does; Shepherd hands a session a task string and walks away. The Jinja2/Handlebars engine and a full JSON-Schema-style typed-input validator are heavy machinery for what is, in Shepherd's world, "fill 2-3 placeholders in a saved task line." Adopting the file format (YAML frontmatter + .prompt files + set_global_prompt_directory) verbatim would duplicate cc-templates.json/cc-presets.json with a parallel store and a YAML parser Shepherd doesn't have (jq is the only hard dep; no YAML). There's also real risk of over-engineering a single-user GUI: dotprompt's output-schema and provider-agnostic model targeting are multi-provider/structured-output concerns Shepherd doesn't need. A strict reading says "you already have templates AND presets — this is redundant."
- **Ruling:** DONT_ADD is wrong because the underlying NEED (parameterized, validated, reusable agent profiles) is genuinely unmet and is the user's explicitly-weighted top future interest — today's templates are verbatim text with no variables and no validation, and presets carry no task body. ADD-as-is is wrong because the dotprompt machinery (completion hooking, merge_messages, compile_prompt, ignore-flags, YAML/Jinja2) is proxy request-shaping for a thing Shepherd never does. The honest verdict is ADAPT: lift the two portable ideas — variable interpolation in saved task text + a typed/required input schema validated before spawn — and graft them onto the EXISTING cc-templates/cc-presets/spawn path rather than importing a parallel .prompt subsystem. Reshape, don't copy: a small pure cc-core function set (parse {{var}} placeholders → required/optional field list → validate supplied vars → render task body), wired into the New Session modal and the queue (which already accepts templates). Reuse the preset's provider/permMode/effort fields as the "model/params the profile supplies." Keep JSON (no YAML/Jinja2 dependency), keep it off-by-default-safe by simply being inert until a profile is invoked. This delivers the user's "saved agents in a directory" goal using Shepherd's own primitives.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend the existing operator-data primitives, do NOT add a parallel .prompt store. (1) cc-core.lua saved-task-templates block (M.templateList/templatePush/templateGet, ~line 3577): add pure helpers M.templateVars(text) → ordered list of {{name}} / {{name?}} placeholders with required/optional flags, and M.renderTemplate(text, vars) → (rendered, missingRequired) that refuses to render on a missing required var. Store an optional vars-schema alongside name/text in cc-templates.json. (2) cc-core.lua spawn-presets block (M.presetList/presetPush, ~line 3640): treat the preset as the "agent profile" carrier — its existing provider/permMode/effort fields ARE dotprompt's "definition supplies the model/params"; optionally link a preset to a template name so a profile = {folder, editor, permMode, provider, templateName}. (3) Wire into M.spawnSpec(editor, project, task, opts) (~line 3061): the task string is the render output. (4) UI: the New Session modal (#newsession in claude-dashboard.lua) prompts for the template's required vars before enabling spawn; the queue path (already template-aware) reuses the same render. Net new code is pure, unit-testable cc-core functions plus modal fields — no YAML parser, no Jinja2, no API plumbing.
- **Skeptic check:** Refutation attempt fails; ADAPT (high) holds. Both downgrade traps were checked against the actual code and neither lands. NOT "already have it": Verified in cc-core.lua — templateList/templatePush store only {name,text} (line 3590) and the dashboard's templateInsert (claude-dashboard.lua:3476) just dumps verbatim text into the nudge box. Presets store only {name,folder,editor,permMode,provider} (presetList, line 3658) with NO task body. A grep for {{ / placeholder / interpolat / inputSchema / handlebars / jinja across both Lua files returns nothing but unrelated HTML input placeholders. There is zero variable-interpolation or schema-validation machinery today, so this is not a re-add. NOT a proxy feature in disguise: The full dotprompt feature IS proxy request-shaping — merge_messages (blend template+client messages), ignore_prompt_manager_model / ignore_prompt_manager_optional_params flags, sampling-param overrides (temperature/top_p), YAML frontmatter + Jinja2/Handlebars, and completion-hooking all presuppose Shepherd building the API request. Shepherd explicitly does NOT proxy or call any API (hard scope limit); it spawns GUI Claude Code sessions and reads ~/.claude/cc-status status files. The judge already STRIPPED all of that and kept only two provider-agnostic ideas: (a) {{var}} interpolation in saved task text, and (b) a typed/required input schema validated BEFORE the task string is dispatched. Those operate on the task string Shepherd already sends via spawnSpec(editor,project,task,opts) (line 3061) and queuePush (line 1858) — a legitimate de-proxied local adaptation, not a disguise. NOT trivial enough to collapse to a plain template extension (ADD): the surviving idea needs a new pure function set in cc-core (parse {{name}}/{{name?}} → required/optional list; render-or-refuse on missing required), a vars-schema persisted alongside name/text, and new New Session modal fields that gate spawn on the required vars. You cannot drop dotprompt in as-is, and the need is real and is the user's explicitly top-weighted future interest ('pre-configured agents we can hand work off to, saved into a directory'). That is the textbook ADAPT zone — reshape onto Shepherd's own primitives (cc-templates/cc-presets/spawnSpec/queue), no YAML, no Jinja2, no API plumbing — exactly what the judge prescribed. Verdict unchanged: ADAPT (high).

#### Prompt management API (CRUD + versioning + environments + scoping + playground/test)  `[H/M]`
- **For:** The underlying need — a saved, reusable, named library of prompt/task strings you hand to a session — is genuinely real for Shepherd and directly on the user's stated future-interest line ("pre-configured agents/skills/MCPs saved into a directory; we haven't leveraged those well enough"). Shepherd ALREADY ships the local seed of this: "Saved task templates" (cc-templates.json: list/save/delete, capped at 50, name+text, replace-in-place on same name, inserted into the nudge/spawn input, never auto-sends; core.templateList/templatePush/templateRemove/templateGet) and "Spawn presets" (cc-presets.json: named {folder,editor,permMode,provider} bundles + per-project last-used). The LiteLLM feature is a more mature version of that exact registry. Two of its sub-ideas have honest local value: (1) lightweight versioning/duplicate-on-edit so an operator doesn't silently destroy a working prompt when they tweak it — templatePush currently overwrites in place; (2) the .prompt->JSON convenience for importing a prompt file. These slot cleanly into the existing template/preset CRUD and the established "operator data lives in a separate JSON outside the Settings round-trip" pattern.
- **Against:** Almost all of the specific mechanism is multi-tenant-proxy plumbing that contradicts Shepherd's hard limits. /prompts/test "renders + forces stream=True through the normal completion path" — i.e. it makes an LLM API call, which Shepherd NEVER does (no proxy, no network, no API calls; it drives GUI sessions, not HTTP routes). The metadata['prompts'] per-API-key allow-list is RBAC for many users over a network; admin-only writes via role checks assume multi-user auth — Shepherd is single-user with no auth. The @@unique([prompt_id,version,environment]) composite key and dev/staging/prod promotion is team release machinery with no single-user-macOS analog. PromptRepository is a SQL store and the in-memory-registry-reinit-on-write is server internals — Shepherd already has the right local persistence (small capped JSON files). Build the as-is feature and you import a database, an environment/promotion model, a key-scoped RBAC filter, and a live completion endpoint, none of which belong in a local console. The genuinely useful residue is small and already 80% built.
- **Ruling:** Not ADD: the feature as specified is proxy-shaped and its headline capability (the streamed /prompts/test playground) directly violates the "no API calls / no network" hard scope limit, while environments + per-key allow-lists + RBAC + SQL are all multi-tenant concerns with no single-user analog. Not DONT_ADD: the core need (a reusable saved-prompt library to hand work to sessions) is real, sits squarely on the user's explicitly high-interest "saved agents/skills" direction, and is ALREADY partially shipped as templates+presets — so this is an extend-what-we-have case, which the rubric calls ADAPT. The reshape: add optional immutable versioning (duplicate-on-edit instead of templatePush's silent in-place overwrite, with a v1/v2 history and revert) to the existing template registry, and import via the .prompt->JSON converter; DROP environments, the key allow-list, RBAC, and SQL; and replace the streamed test endpoint with Shepherd's existing primitive — render the template with sample vars and PASTE it into a chosen live session without auto-sending (the human runs the test, exactly matching the "templates never auto-send" contract). This keeps it local, single-user, no-network, and reuses the operator-data-JSON pattern.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend the existing Saved task templates registry: cc-core.lua M.templateList/templatePush/templateRemove/templateGet (~line 3577) backed by cc-templates.json, surfaced via the "Tpl ▾" button in claude-dashboard.lua (ccTemplates/renderTemplates/templateInsert) and the template-list/save/delete actions in handleAction. Add a version field + duplicate-on-edit (a new templatePushVersioned that prepends v(n+1) rather than overwriting in place) and a versions/revert view; add the .prompt->JSON import as a new FX reader feeding templatePush. The "test" maps to the existing no-auto-send paste path (handleAction nudge/spawn input insertion) — render sample vars locally, paste into a selected session, human submits. Persist as operator data outside the Settings round-trip, same as cc-templates.json/cc-presets.json/cc-labels.json. Explicitly OMIT: environments/promotion, metadata['prompts'] allow-list, admin role checks, the SQL PromptRepository, and the streamed /prompts/test completion call.
- **Skeptic check:** Tried to refute as both DONT_ADD ("already have it") and ADD; neither sticks, so ADAPT holds. Verified against the code: Shepherd already ships a saved-prompt library — M.templateList/templatePush/templateRemove/templateGet (cc-core.lua:3590-3638) backed by cc-templates.json, surfaced via the "Tpl ▾" button + ccTemplates/renderTemplates/templateInsert/template-save/-delete in claude-dashboard.lua (~3453-3495, handleAction ~1884). templateInsert sets el.value and focuses the nudge box — it never auto-sends (claude-dashboard.lua:3480), which is exactly the "test = render + paste, human submits" reshape the judge proposed; that path already exists. So the FEATURE's library + no-auto-send-test core is partially already present, which argues against ADD. But it is NOT fully present: templatePush overwrites a same-name entry IN PLACE (cc-core.lua:3613) — there is no versioning, no v1/v2 history, no revert (grep for version/revert/immutable on templates: none), and there is no variable interpolation/render-with-sample-vars in cc-core, and no .prompt importer. Those are real, locally-coherent gaps that sit squarely on the user's explicitly high-interest "saved agents/skills" direction. That is a legitimate extension, so DONT_ADD ("already have it") is too harsh. The full FEATURE is plainly proxy-shaped and the judge correctly stripped it: the headline /prompts/test STREAMS an LLM completion, which directly violates Shepherd's hard "NO network server / does NOT proxy or make API calls" limit (context.md:13); and environments (dev/staging/prod promotion via composite key), per-key metadata['prompts'] allow-lists, RBAC ("config-defined prompts protected" = admin role), and the SQL PromptRepository are all multi-tenant concerns with no single-user analog. The judge dropped every one of those and kept only what bolts onto the existing template registry: optional immutable duplicate-on-edit versioning + revert/history, a .prompt->JSON FX importer, and local sample-var rendering paste-tested via the existing no-auto-send nudge path. That residue is an extension of an existing primitive, not a new capability area — which is precisely what ADAPT means in this rubric. Verdict unchanged: ADAPT (the streamed test + environments + allow-list + RBAC + SQL must be omitted, exactly as the mapping states).

#### Remote prompt sources (git / SaaS prompt registries)  `[H/M]`
- **For:** The underlying need — reusable, versioned prompt/agent definitions that live somewhere durable instead of being retyped per session — is exactly the user's stated high-interest area: "pre-configured agents we can hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs." Shepherd already has the seed of this: cc-templates.json (saved task prompts) and cc-presets.json (saved spawn bundles: folder+editor+permMode+provider), and prompt text already flows cleanly into a session via spawnInner's [prompt] arg, the queue, and feedTask. A "load prompt/agent definition from a source" layer is a natural extension of that existing primitive. The git-repo variant in particular is close: a saved-agents/skills/prompts *directory* is just a versioned folder, and a developer-grade source like that fits a single-user workflow where you want the same agent profile across machines (Shepherd already rsync-mirrors state over the SSH bridge).
- **Against:** As-built, every concrete integration LiteLLM ships violates a hard scope limit. Langfuse, Arize Phoenix, and the SaaS registries are network calls to a remote service and require an API key — Shepherd has NO network server and NO secrets storage (it never stores keys, only resolves $ENV via the spawned shell). The Bitbucket/GitLab sources take workspace+repo+token+branch — that is credential storage plus network fetch. The whole abstraction exists in LiteLLM so a multi-tenant proxy can centralize prompt strings and inject them into outbound API calls server-side; Shepherd does not proxy or make API calls, so the "fetch a prompt and splice it into a request" mechanism has no home. Building the auto-discovery initializer registry and the per-provider clients would import proxy machinery (tokens, HTTP clients, provider SDKs) that the architecture deliberately excludes.
- **Ruling:** The remote/SaaS/token-bearing implementation is a clean DONT_ADD against three hard limits (no network server, no secrets, no API proxy), and the LiteLLM mechanism (inject prompt string into an outbound request) has no analog in a console that only reads local status files and drives GUI sessions. But the NEED maps directly onto the user's top-priority future interest (saved agents/skills/MCPs in a directory), and Shepherd already owns the local half of it (templates + presets feeding spawn). So this is ADAPT, not DONT_ADD: keep the idea of a reusable prompt/agent-definition *source*, but reshape it to a LOCAL filesystem source — a configurable directory of prompt/agent-profile files read off disk — and drop every networked/credentialed provider. That extends presets/templates from flat JSON entries into a browsable local registry that can seed a spawn's prompt, system-prompt, or MCP/skill config, with zero network and zero secrets. The git angle, if ever wanted, is satisfied by the user simply keeping that directory under version control (and it already rides the SSH-mirror), not by Shepherd holding a repo token.
- **How (Shepherd):** Extend the existing local-registry pair: cc-templates.json (M.templateList/Push/Get) and cc-presets.json (M.presetList/Push in cc-core.lua ~3577-3710). Add a local "prompt/agent-definition source" = a configurable directory (e.g. ~/.claude/agents or a user-set folder) scanned with the existing folder-scan accelerators (core.folderScanShellCommand + resolveBin rg/fd, graceful grep/find fallback). A pure cc-core loader parses each file into a {name, prompt/systemPrompt, presetFields} record that feeds the New Session modal and spawnInner's [prompt] arg (plus spawnFlags for any --append-system-prompt/--add-dir/MCP wiring). Strictly local-disk read; no network client, no token storage. Surface chips in the spawn modal alongside today's preset chips.
- **Skeptic check:** ADAPT survives scrutiny; I could not refute it. Trap (a) does not apply: the literal LiteLLM feature is a credentialed network client (Bitbucket/GitLab workspace+repo+token+branch, Langfuse prompt_label/version, Phoenix) and is a clean DONT_ADD against all three hard limits (no network server, no secrets, no API proxy) — the judge already stripped every networked/token-bearing provider, so what remains is genuinely local-disk-only, not a proxy dressed up as local. Trap (b) does not apply either: I verified Shepherd does NOT already have this. templateList/Push/Get (cc-core ~3590) are named text snippets that get inserted into the queue input via the Tpl button (dashboard:3107) — never used to seed a spawn prompt. presetList/Push (cc-core ~3658) is a {name, folder, editor, permMode, provider} bundle carrying NO prompt, NO system-prompt, NO MCP/skill config; the New Session modal's "Initial task" textarea (n-task, dashboard:3263) is hand-typed and not saved into a preset. grep found no existing agent-profile / prompt-source / skills-registry / MCP-management feature anywhere. The adapted feature — a configurable LOCAL directory scanned with the existing fd/rg accelerators (folderScanShellCommand + resolveBin, graceful grep/find fallback) into {name, prompt, systemPrompt, presetFields} records that seed the spawn modal — is mechanically supported (spawnInner already takes a prompt arg; spawnFlags already emits launch flags so --append-system-prompt/--add-dir wiring is feasible) and lands squarely on the user's verbatim #1 future interest: "Pre-configured agents we can hand work off to, saved into a directory; same with skills and MCPs." This is a real extension of presets/templates into a browsable local agent-definition SOURCE, not a re-add. ADD was never warranted (the literal remote/token feature is dead); ADAPT (high) is the correct verdict and it holds.

### Health & Ops

#### Active callbacks / settings introspection (GET /active/callbacks, /settings)  `[H/S]`
- **For:** The underlying NEED — "let the operator confirm the thing is actually wired correctly before trusting it" — is real and recurring in Shepherd. A spawned Claude Code session is only useful to the panel if its hooks (cc-status.sh / cc-approve.sh / cc-popup.sh) are merged into ~/.claude/settings.json and writing status JSON; if they aren't, the operator gets a silent half-broken tile with no explicit "why." Shepherd already has the pieces LiteLLM's endpoint conceptually mirrors: `make doctor` (install.sh --tools-only) prints ✅/⚠️/❌ for jq/rg/fd and which engine each path uses, and `core.mergeHooks` knows how to detect whether our hooks are wired. Surfacing that same introspection live in the panel (hooks present? gate armed? ledger writable? which search/scan engine is hot? config last-loaded?) is a natural, in-scope extension that turns a CLI-only check into a glanceable Health/Ops readout.
- **Against:** The literal LiteLLM feature is a multi-tenant proxy operator's introspection over an HTTP route, returning constructs Shepherd has no equivalent of: a registered callbacks list (success/failure/input logging plugins), an alerting service config, and request_timeout. Shepherd makes no API calls, has no proxy, no callbacks registry, no alerting daemon, and no request timeout — so the payload itself is meaningless here. And the "confirm wiring" need is already substantially served: `make doctor` reports tool/engine status, install.sh prints ✅/⚠️ wiring lines, mergeHooks guarantees idempotent hook installation, and the ⚙ Settings panel already shows the dark cc-config.json. One could argue the gap (CLI vs panel) is too thin to justify new build, i.e. "already have it."
- **Ruling:** The HTTP/callbacks/alerting/request_timeout shape is pure proxy plumbing and DONT_ADD as-is. But the abstracted need — operator confirmation that the local wiring is healthy — is genuine and only partially covered today: it lives at the CLI/install layer (make doctor, mergeHooks) and not in the live console, even though a session can be running with un-wired hooks and produce a silently degraded tile. That makes this an extension of an existing capability rather than a new concern, which is the definition of ADAPT. The honest scope is small: a read-only "wiring/health" strip in the panel reusing functions that already exist (mergeHooks' detection logic, the tooling_check engine probe, gate-armed and ledger-writable state). No network, no secrets, no new daemon. It earns ADAPT, not ADD (the source shape doesn't transfer) and not DONT_ADD (the CLI-only coverage leaves a real in-panel blind spot).
- **How (Shepherd):** Health & Ops: add a pure cc-core helper (e.g. core.wiringStatus) that, given parsed settings.json + tooling probe results + current config/gate/ledger state, returns a struct {hooksWired (reusing the mergeHooks/OUR_HOOK_SCRIPTS detection), searchEngine/scanEngine (rg|grep, fd|find from install.sh tooling_check), jqPresent, gateArmed, ledgerWritable, configLoadedAt}. Surface it in the ⚙ Settings panel (a "Diagnostics / Health" section) and reuse the same struct as the body of `make doctor`. No new side-effect channel needed — it reads filesystem state already read by the refresh loop; per Shepherd's split, logic in cc-core.lua, glue in claude-dashboard.lua, panel-JS mirror only if rendered live.
- **Skeptic check:** The ADAPT (high) ruling survives both refutation tests. (a) Not a proxy feature in disguise: the source shape (GET /active/callbacks, /settings — callbacks, alerting, request_timeout) is pure multi-tenant proxy plumbing and the judge already rejected it DONT_ADD-as-is. The mapping it proposes is genuinely local/single-user — read settings.json + filesystem state already read by the 1s refresh loop, no network, no secrets, no daemon. The abstracted need ("is my local wiring healthy?") carries over; the proxy mechanics do not. (b) Not already present in the relevant surface: every primitive the mapping reuses exists, but ONLY at the CLI/console layer, not in the live panel. Verified: `make doctor` -> install.sh `tooling_check` (jq required; rg/fd accelerators) is install/CLI-only (install.sh:38, Makefile:29); `mergeHooks`/`M.OUR_HOOK_SCRIPTS={cc-status,cc-approve,cc-popup}.sh` hook-wiring detection lives in cc-core.lua:2562; the rg|grep search-engine kind already exists (cc-core.lua:825/838) but is only `print`-logged to the runtime console (claude-dashboard.lua:880, todos.md:213). The ⚙ Settings overlay (claude-dashboard.lua:3119-3235) is a pure config FORM with NO read-only diagnostics/health section — grep for diagnostic/health/wiringStatus/jqPresent/ledgerWritable in both Lua files returns nothing. The blind spot is real and confirmed by the empty state ("Waiting for Claude Code sessions...", claude-dashboard.lua:3038/4977), which is indistinguishable between 'no sessions' and 'session running but hooks un-wired'; todos.md:215 even names this failure class ("exactly how fd went uninstalled for a while"). So: ADD fails (source shape is proxy plumbing, doesn't transfer); DONT_ADD fails (CLI/console-only coverage leaves a genuine in-panel blind spot); it is an extension of an existing capability into a surface that lacks it — the definition of ADAPT. Honest scope is small and fits the architecture split: a pure cc-core helper (e.g. core.wiringStatus) returning {hooksWired, searchEngine/scanEngine, jqPresent, gateArmed, ledgerWritable, configLoadedAt}, surfaced as a read-only Diagnostics section in ⚙ and reused as the body of `make doctor`. Recommendation: ADD a short clarification to the panel-JS mirror caveat (only render live if it reads state the refresh loop already gathers; don't add a new probe channel) — otherwise the mapping is sound. Verdict holds at ADAPT.

#### Test a single model/provider connection (POST /health/test_connection)  `[L/M]`
- **For:** The underlying need is real and lands squarely on a primitive Shepherd already owns: gateway provider profiles (cc-core.lua:2618+, kind="gateway" sets ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL to a LiteLLM-style gateway with $AUTH_TOKEN env). Today a wrong base_url/model/auth in a profile fails SILENTLY — you don't learn until you spawn a session and it errors. Shepherd already carries scar tissue from this: respawnSpec returns canRespawn=false reason="unknown gateway provider", and the whole Auto-Continue bounded loop-guard exists because dead connections happen. A "Test provider profile" preflight (does this profile actually reach a live endpoint with the right model?) would close that gap before the operator spawns N sessions against a broken gateway, and it directly serves the user's stated interest in better-leveraged saved provider/agent configs. It also composes with the existing make-doctor "what's live" idea, extending it from local CLI tools to configured gateways.
- **Against:** The LiteLLM feature as built violates Shepherd's three hardest scope limits at once: it is an HTTP endpoint (NO network server), the proxy itself MAKES THE API CALL (Shepherd explicitly "does NOT proxy or make API calls"), and it accepts overridden/ad-hoc credentials (NO secrets storage — Shepherd never sees a key, it only emits $VAR refs the spawned login shell expands). Building any version means net-new machinery Shepherd has zero of: a network/reachability fx effect, request timeout, and provider-specific error parsing, where today the only health check (make doctor) just probes for local binaries. For a single-user who hand-configures a handful of profiles, the payoff is thin: the cheapest existing "test" is to just spawn one session against the profile and watch the tile go error vs working — Auto-Continue and the error state already surface a dead connection.
